kernel/AGENTS.md	- All changes must pass `AIENOS_STRICT=1 ./scripts/verify_all.sh` with zero warnings (`cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, clean `cargo fmt`).
kernel/AGENTS.md	Only copy to caller output buffer after tag verification passes.
kernel/AGENTS.md	This document codifies mandatory architectural invariants, cryptographic boundaries, and coding standards for all agents modifying `aienos-recovery-gate`.
kernel/AUDIT_SCOPE.md	- A head that changes `.aien/`, `tests/gates/` or `.github/workflows/` is run by the operator only under the shared AIEN pipeline rules; this repository has no pipeline document of its own.
kernel/AUDIT_SCOPE.md	All: NOT_RUN while writing this document.
kernel/AUDIT_SCOPE.md	Every gate is `tests/gates/run.sh <step>`; steps include `ck-host-tests`, `ck-stage-tests`, `ck-gate-selftest`, `ck-hold-selftest`, `ck-owner-keys`, `ck-qemu-gates`, `ck-qemu-external` (fails unless `ALLEN_MODEL`, `LOCAL_MODEL_WEIGHTS` and `LOCAL_MODEL_TOKENIZER` are given), `ck-b7b-probe`, `native-net`, `native-sig`, `native-crypto`, `native-m5`, `native-disk`, `native-argus`, `native-capability`, `disk-qemu`, `crypto-boundary`, `infer-crate`, `store-native`, `verify-all` and `zero-disk-secrets`.
kernel/AUDIT_SCOPE.md	Pins: `omega.lock` names omega commit `362c9ab50c8ecc3012dd2e3ac20329865ba80982` and the sha256 of the vendored `native/kernel/third_party/omega/rx_cortex.c` and `rx_cortex.h`; `crumb.lock` (history only since DRA-97, 2026-11-07; no gate reads it) names `d0f0b95d344b4c6628c3d868346932fd247ce168`.
kernel/AUDIT_SCOPE.md	## Run every test
kernel/AUDIT_SCOPE.md	The C kernel is IMPLEMENTED / NOT QUALIFIED; its emulator gates pass in QEMU only.
kernel/CONTEXT.md	**Qualification Trust Anchor**: An explicitly test-only public key admitted for SEED-0B qualification; it conveys no production owner or machine identity.
kernel/CONTRIBUTING.md	Crumbs are retired from the active workflow (DRA-97, 2026-11-07): no gate or workflow checks them, and reading, writing or refreshing a crumb is not a step of any change.

kernel/CONTRIBUTORS.md	The orchestrator checks all output before it merges.
kernel/crates/aienos-anchor/README.md	Removing, reordering or editing any entry breaks the chain, and a broken log is refused whole.

kernel/crates/aienos-anchor/src/lib.rs	Any flaw refuses the whole log: a tampered history is never partly trusted.
kernel/crates/aienos-artifact/src/receipt.rs	Its seed exists only in debug host qualification tooling and tests.

kernel/crates/aienos-artifact-tool/README.md	That feature uses only the public RFC 8032 test-vector identity.
kernel/crates/aienos-artifact-tool/src/corpus.rs	Debug builds with `seed0b-test-signing` only; never part of the kernel or release tooling.
kernel/crates/aienos-artifact-tool/src/main.rs	Test fixture only; never use as a secret.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/handoff.rs	Each part gets its own buffer (16 grant lines alone can exceed 2 KiB), and a part that still overflows is reported, never dropped silently: the host fails any run that shows `report-truncated:`.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/handoff.rs	The post-handoff firmware variable write is a temporary bootstrap exception (ADR 0008): versioned name, at most `MAX_VAR_WRITES` writes of at most `MAX_VAR_BYTES` each, diagnostics only, and a failed write never stops boot.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/nvme_read.rs	Mode 9 inspects and prints the challenge for the applicable action; modes 10/11 verify the operator response in control-block bytes 16..48 and perform the action only if it verifies for exactly this state.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/nvme_read.rs	Recovery Core qualification (ADR 0006) with the TEST ONLY operator key.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/nvme_read.rs	SAFETY: `base` is the NVMe BAR the PCI takeover assigned, covered by the identity map, and the driver passes only NVMe register offsets.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/nvme_read.rs	--------------------------------------------------------------------------- Store-over-NVMe qualification (test-only, `store-qual`).
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/store_qual.rs	Fixed test-only Store UUID used by the qualification images.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/store_qual.rs	TEST ONLY operator key for the QEMU Recovery Core qualification.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/store_qual.rs	Test-only Store-over-NVMe qualification plumbing.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/usb_keyboard.rs	Built only with the `usb-keyboard` feature, which the QEMU keyboard test enables and hardware staging does not: the candidate is proven in QEMU and never admitted to Machine 1 before M3 can confine it.
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/wcslen.rs	Since rustc 1.99 (LLVM 23) the loop-idiom pass turns any "count 16-bit units until zero" loop into a call to `wcslen`, and the UEFI targets ship no C library to provide it (rust-lang/rust#160827, #163614).
kernel/crates/aienos-boot/src/wcslen.rs	The `uefi` crate has such loops (`proto::shell`, `proto::driver::component_name`), so every image that links it fails with `undefined symbol: wcslen`.
kernel/crates/aienos-capability-ffi/src/lib.rs	A caller that checks this report cannot mistake one for the other.
kernel/crates/aienos-capability-ffi/src/lib.rs	The new table starts above every generation the old one used (fix mirrored from the native authority, b1fa747), so outstanding references fail against it even after reclaims.
kernel/crates/aienos-capability/src/lib.rs	A restart passes [`AuthorityState::generation_floor`] of the table it replaces, so the new table starts above every generation the old one handed out, including generations a reclaim moved past the old boot generation.
kernel/crates/aienos-capability/src/lib.rs	Every free slot exhausted: mint refuses rather than reuse one.
kernel/crates/aienos-capability/src/lib.rs	Refuses a live slot, so it cannot mint a forged generation.
kernel/crates/aienos-capability/src/lib.rs	Those operations live on [`AuthorityAdmin`], and they refuse to run unless the caller still holds the office token that was never written into the table.
kernel/crates/aienos-crypto/src/aes.rs	Reference Rijndael S-box from FIPS 197 Figure 7, used only to check `sub_bytes_ct`.
kernel/crates/aienos-evidence/src/main.rs	Exits 0 PASS, 1 FAIL, 3 BLOCKED, 2 when a capture cannot be read or parsed (stdout, as the former verifier printed).
kernel/crates/aienos-evidence/src/main.rs	Usage errors exit 2, never 1, so they cannot be mistaken for a FAIL verdict.
kernel/crates/aienos-evidence/src/rollback.rs	Exit status: 0 PASS, 1 FAIL, 3 BLOCKED, and 2 when a capture cannot be read or does not have the capture shape.
kernel/crates/aienos-explain/src/adapters.rs	Fails with `NotInBranchScope` when the record is neither referenced by the branch nor in the support ancestry of a referenced record, so unrelated Cortex facts cannot be pulled in to strengthen a branch.
kernel/crates/aienos-explain/src/adapters.rs	`lookup` is any read-only view of the store (the real `CortexStore`, an imported journal, a test map).
kernel/crates/aienos-explain/src/adapters.rs	The seal is checked; the live store is never touched.
kernel/crates/aienos-fpu-probe/src/lib.rs	Inputs go through black_box so LLVM cannot constant-fold the FP work away (the gate must execute real FP/SIMD instructions).
kernel/crates/aienos-infer/examples/model_probe.rs	The prompt is given as token ids (the `ids` line printed by `tests/ref/llama_ref.c`), so the engine's own tokenizer is NOT exercised: this judges only the weights loader, the quant paths and the transformer forward pass.
kernel/crates/aienos-infer-kernel/src/lib.rs	Calling it again with `(0, 0)` after the kernel has returned the frames forgets the region: every later allocation fails closed (null -> panic) instead of touching freed memory.
kernel/crates/aienos-infer-kernel/src/lib.rs	Fields are only meaningful up to the stage that failed.
kernel/crates/aienos-infer-kernel/src/lib.rs	Unload: the kernel returns the region's frames and calls `aienos_infer_heap_init(0, 0)`, after which every allocation fails closed.
kernel/crates/aienos-infer-kernel/src/session.rs	The allocator never reclaims old blocks, so a long run of prompts creeps upward; `heap_reserve` turns that creep into a refusal (`E_HEAP`) before any allocation can fail, and the kernel can read the creep (`aienos_infer_heap_stats`) and show it.
kernel/crates/aienos-infer/src/dotprod.rs	Block dot with the portable integer arithmetic (reference for the tests; also the only path on non-aarch64).
kernel/crates/aienos-infer/src/dotprod.rs	Every SDOT function checks nothing itself; it is only reachable with a token.
kernel/crates/aienos-infer/src/dotprod.rs	On non-aarch64 targets no token can be made and the portable integer reference below is the only implementation (it is also what the tests compare the SDOT code with).
kernel/crates/aienos-infer/src/dotprod.rs	Selection is a typed capability: a [`DotProd`] token exists only when the caller said the CPU has dotprod (the kernel passes the decode of `ID_AA64ISAR0_EL1.DP`, never assumed here).
kernel/crates/aienos-infer/src/lib.rs	Every read is length checked and hostile input yields a [`GgufError`], never a panic.
kernel/crates/aienos-infer/src/quant.rs	d=1, all scales 1, ql nibbles = 0x0, qh = 0 -> q = -32 everywhere for low nibbles; set specific positions to check the bit layout.
kernel/crates/aienos-infer/tests/fixtures/PROVENANCE.md	Regenerate: make -C crates/aienos-infer/tests/fixtures/gen regen Generator is C only (gen/gen.c, no Python).
kernel/crates/aienos-infer/tests/golden.rs	Round to nearest, normal range only (test scales are well inside it).
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/abi.rs	Decoding fails closed: short or long input, reserved bits or fields that are not zero, unknown discriminants, unknown versions and generation-zero handles are all rejected with a distinct [`AbiError`].
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/abi.rs	Fails with [`AbiError::ReservedBits`] if any bit 6 to 31 is set.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/arch/aarch64.rs	SAFETY: `base_addr` is the UART register block this driver was created for, and every caller passes a `uart16550_regs` offset inside it.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/artifact_loader.rs	Every reason a candidate can be refused or torn down.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/artifact_loader.rs	Every record passes the strict §7 rules before it is returned; nothing is filled from expectations.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/artifact_loader.rs	Fail-closed W^X audit of the sealed task root: - the user window holds exactly the expected leaves (code EL0 RO+X, data and stack EL0 RW+NX, guards unmapped) and nothing else; - every code frame's identity alias is read-only and never executable; - no user-window leaf is both writable and executable.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/artifact_loader.rs	Hardware access goes through [`LoaderPlatform`] so the whole pipeline, including every rollback, runs under host tests.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/artifact_loader_tests.rs	A non-zero self-check exit never counts as a passed canary.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/artifact_loader_tests.rs	Mapped-byte corruption: authorised (signer and grants bound), refused at Hashed with the loader reason, never executed.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/artifact_loader_tests.rs	TEST ONLY.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/artifact_loader_tests.rs	With every loaded-task slot taken, the resource snapshot offers no pages, so admission refuses before anything is reserved.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/block.rs	Every write from that point onward fails.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity.rs	An overflowing sum can never equal `children` (<= 255), so it is refused with the same class and text as the C codec (cc_state_validate in native/kernel/svc/continuity_codec.c).
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity.rs	A table that passes the fork-sum check below has every forks <= 255, so the cap changes no outcome for a valid table; an id past the cap is refused here as Corrupt("branch lineage is inconsistent"), same as C.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity.rs	Continuity objects without their root mean the identity was lost, not that the store was never provisioned: fail toward preservation.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity.rs	D-1/D-2 golden-vector emitter for the C codec (test-only).
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity.rs	Only reachable from an explicit, authorised provisioning request; refuses if any root exists.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity.rs	Strict reader: every read is bounds-checked and the whole input must be consumed.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity_vectors.rs	* `continuity_verdicts.txt`: for every vector, the Rust decode verdict of the untouched bytes and of every single-bit flip (D-2).
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity_vectors.rs	Every decode refusal text continuity.rs can return, as (code, class, why).
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity_vectors.rs	Regenerate (the only command): AIENOS_CONTINUITY_VECTORS_REGEN=1 cargo test -p aienos-kernel --lib continuity_vectors Without the variable the test fails if a committed fixture differs from freshly emitted bytes, so the fixtures cannot drift from the oracle.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/continuity_vectors.rs	The C host test (`native/kernel/tests/test_continuity_codec.c`) reads both files and must reproduce every byte, every ObjectId and every verdict.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/crypto/envelope.rs	Exactly at the bound is allowed by the size check, but the claimed length cannot match the short input, so the checked length comparison rejects it.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/crypto/envelope.rs	The header fields are unauthenticated at this point, so every step is checked and an overflow returns `LengthOverflow` instead of wrapping or panicking.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	A device can reach memory only through two gates that the kernel controls:
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	A table missing from `tables` counts as gone, so the caller must pass every live table.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	`CapTable::revoke` checks the derivation tree itself and can still refuse after the hardware is down (only on malformed derivation data).
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	Every authority check happens before any hardware effect.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	Every check runs before any hardware effect, so a refused request touches no hardware.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	If a hardware step fails, the remaining steps still run, the capability is still revoked, the device is left in [`DmaState::Revoking`] so it cannot be activated, and the first hardware error is returned.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	It opens the IOMMU gate first and the BME gate second, so there is never a moment where the device can issue DMA that the IOMMU would not confine.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	Records every hardware effect and can fail one chosen step.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	Removing authority never needs authority, so the kernel may call this at any time (for example to retry after a failed teardown left the device in [`DmaState::Revoking`]).
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	Revocation closes the gates in the reverse order: stop handing the device new work, clear BME, quiesce or reset the device, set the stream to ABORT, invalidate any cached translations, and only then remove the capability and everything derived from it.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	Taking authority away never depends on the device cooperating: if a hardware step fails, the remaining steps still run, so BME ends clear and the stream ends aborted wherever the hardware allows, and the error is reported.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	These checks run before any hardware effect.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/device.rs	This module is the only code that opens those gates, and it opens them only when a capability that names the device and carries the `MAP` right is presented.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/dma_gate.rs	A present SMMU that failed to initialise never falls back to the bypass.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/fatal.rs	The decoding is pure and host-tested; the vectors and register reads exist only on AArch64.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/gic.rs	SAFETY: `base` is the GIC register frame mapped at its MADT address, and the programming code passes only GICD/GICR offsets inside it.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/infer/gemm.rs	SAFETY: caller-facing shape checks prove every accessed element is in bounds.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/ipc.rs	SAFETY: EL1-only state; bounds checked above.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/ipc.rs	The guard releases on drop so a panicking test cannot wedge the rest.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/net.rs	All packet offsets are checked.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/nvme/atomicity.rs	A write is power-fail atomic only if it fits entirely inside that unit AND does not cross an atomic boundary (`NABSPF`/`NABO`).
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/nvme/atomicity.rs	If the guarantee cannot be established, qualification fails closed.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/recovery_core.rs	Failure semantics fail toward preservation (ADR 0007): - repair only a *malformed* peer, and only when the valid root still resolves the identity, so the repair cannot lose it; - never discard a CRC-valid newer root whose graph is broken: that would roll back committed state, so it is an out-of-band decision; - provision only on a healthy (`Valid`) mount with no continuity objects at all.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/recovery_core.rs	Refused on any other state.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/recovery_core_tests.rs	A response that is genuinely valid for this exact state still cannot provision or repair: the refusal is the preservation rule, not the key.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/recovery.rs	It is prepended to the challenge inside the HMAC input so a tag produced for this credential check can never be confused with any other HMAC use of the same key.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/recovery.rs	where `OPERATOR_AUTH_DOMAIN` is `b"AIENOS-RECOVERY-OPERATOR-AUTH-v1\0"` (33 bytes), and accepts only if `expected` equals `response`, compared with [`constant_time_eq`] so the check does not exit early on the first differing byte.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/smmu.rs	CD word 0 carries the TTBR0/TTBR1 configuration and every control bit: T0SZ, TG0, IRGN0, ORGN0, SH0, EPD0, EPD1, V, IPS, AA64, R, A and ASID.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/smmu.rs	Order, fail closed at every step: 1.

kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/task_runtime.rs	Generation 0 never parses; wrong generation and wrong index fail lookup.
kernel/crates/aienos-kernel/src/task_runtime.rs	The loader (`artifact_loader`) builds the private address space and the capability table; this module only enters EL0 with that state, dispatches the task's syscalls against its own table, enforces the admitted CPU, elapsed-time and syscall budgets, and returns control to the kernel on exit, fault, timeout, bad syscall, or budget overrun.

kernel/crates/allen-reflex-kernel/src/heap.rs	Empty heap: every allocation fails until [`Bump::init`].

kernel/crates/allen-reflex-kernel/src/kernel.rs	It never returns, so the decision in progress is never printed, never reaches the gate, and nothing is written (the pi5-allen image has no Store stage).
kernel/crates/allen-reflex-kernel/tests/heap_budget.rs	It checks that the run fits, that every decision returns the heap to its mark (repeated decisions do not grow it), that the second pass gives the same lines, and prints the peaks used in the docs.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex-kernel/tests/heap_budget.rs	pass-1 lines kept in buffers allocated before any mark, so no decision leaves a live block
kernel/crates/allen-reflex-kernel/tests/heap_budget.rs	The kernel's run, on the host, under the kernel's own heap: this test installs the same bump heap as the global allocator over a region of the size the kernel hands over (`ALLEN_HEAP_BYTES` in `native/kernel/core/allen.c`) and runs the kernel's sequence: card index, then per mode train + every case twice with the per-decision mark/reset.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/lib.rs	Never fails: every outcome is one schema-valid decision.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/model.rs	Rebuild prototypes from all exemplars: one bundling pass, then retraining passes with the ternary model in the loop (OnlineHD-style weights).
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/policy.rs	Check all clauses; returns the rule and the token where it fired.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/route.rs	A scored `refuse` far from every example also becomes `clarify` (dev: unfamiliar words otherwise scored as refuse); policy refusals returned above are never softened.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/route.rs	Never fails: every outcome is one proposal.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/route.rs	| route | meaning | |---|---| | `local_deterministic` | answered on the Pi by fixed code (status, help, evidence, "I cannot do that") | | `memory` | read of approved owner memory (recall, facts) | | `clarify` | the explicit unknown: ALLEN asks instead of guessing | | `refuse` | the request breaks a rule or a bound | | `local_model` | generation by the model resident on the Pi | | `spark_inference` | generation on the Spark (a PST1 `request` is built, never sent here) | | `spark_omegese` | a bounded Spark task (PST1 `request`, approval required before it may be sent) | | `approval_needed` | the turn asks for an owner decision or a Store write: forwarded to the owner desk of aienos#396 |
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/tower.rs	A receipt from one tier (for example the labelled Pi test tier) never stands in for another tier.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/tower.rs	Decide how to handle `spec` without dropping or weakening any gate check.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/tower.rs	Nothing links this module into the Pi kernel or any test runner yet, and no Pi or Spark run has exercised it.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/src/tower.rs	Rules this core enforces: - A PASS receipt is reused only for the exact `(gate, tier, input_digest)` it was recorded for, and only for gates marked cacheable.
kernel/crates/allen-reflex/tests/core.rs	Where a test checks how a known case is handled, that case and all its paraphrases are left out of memory first, so the answer has to come from generalisation.

kernel/docs/adr/0012-self-construction-capability-growth-and-generations.md	- Evidence rules tighten for agents: a candidate's proof states what was tested (QEMU, host, hardware) and never implies hardware behavior it did not observe.
kernel/docs/adr/0012-self-construction-capability-growth-and-generations.md	**Generations.** Every accepted system state is an immutable, numbered Generation recording: parent, source provenance, build provenance, code hashes, tests, proof receipts, capability grants, hardware target, benchmark evidence, and rollback target.
kernel/docs/adr/0012-self-construction-capability-growth-and-generations.md	**Survival kit, then growth.** Every AIEN has what it needs to exist, recover, and talk with its person: Seed/recovery, identity, console/display, keyboard/input, storage, networking, time, the Forge build/test machinery, audio output, voice synthesis, and a text interface.
kernel/docs/adr/0012-self-construction-capability-growth-and-generations.md	The trusted base supplies mechanisms; each machine grows the capabilities it and its owner need, and every accepted change is a numbered, reversible Generation admitted by deterministic gates.

kernel/docs/adr/0014-binary-artifact-v0-and-native-admission.md	The matching private test key is explicitly TEST ONLY and exists only in the qualification signer/test fixture; it is never embedded in a kernel image, boot media, or ordinary/release tooling defaults.
kernel/docs/adr/0014-binary-artifact-v0-and-native-admission.md	- The qualification gate checks this: the OSC unit gate refuses a TEST-signed unit in an ordinary build, and the boot report names the build as a TEST-only qualification tier.
kernel/docs/adr/0014-binary-artifact-v0-and-native-admission.md	The refusal code follows the spec's fixed check order (its section 8.2): a request on an unsupported domain is refused `CAP_DOMAIN_UNSUPPORTED` (27) first, a domain-1 value wider than 32 bits `CAP_GEN_NOT_REPRESENTABLE` (28) next, and any remaining nonzero pin `CAP_GENERATION_STALE` (29).
kernel/docs/adr/0014-binary-artifact-v0-and-native-admission.md	- This is TEST only.

kernel/docs/adr/0019-per-resource-capability-generation.md	- The kernel's refusal of a nonzero pin is run, not only read: aienos#284 (`fa209e45`) added `a02` to the QEMU gate.
kernel/docs/adr/0019-per-resource-capability-generation.md	The position until then (ADR 0014 amendment point 5, aienos#277) is fail closed: AIENOS supports domain 1 only and refuses every nonzero pin, in the spec's fixed order: `CAP_DOMAIN_UNSUPPORTED` (27), then `CAP_GEN_NOT_REPRESENTABLE` (28) for a domain-1 value above `0xFFFFFFFF`, then `CAP_GENERATION_STALE` (29) for any remaining nonzero pin.

kernel/docs/adr/0019-per-resource-capability-generation.md	What it is: this ADR adopts the incarnation counter of Option 2 as the contract (same rules, same rollback and fail-closed clauses), but the kernel keeps `no_resource` and keeps refusing every nonzero pin.
kernel/docs/adr/0020-pi5-software-only-dma-confinement.md	Descriptor-ring helpers can only emit in-window addresses (property test over arbitrary inputs, including hostile ones).
kernel/docs/adr/0020-pi5-software-only-dma-confinement.md	**Every descriptor checked.** Each descriptor address and length must lie fully inside the window.
kernel/docs/adr/0020-pi5-software-only-dma-confinement.md	Fail closed: any violation stops the NIC, it does not log and continue.
kernel/docs/adr/0020-pi5-software-only-dma-confinement.md	**Label.** The boot banner prints `software-only DMA confinement (Pi 5, weaker than the Spark)`, and every gate that exercises this path carries `SWDMA` in its name.
kernel/docs/adr/0020-pi5-software-only-dma-confinement.md	Label used everywhere (boot banner, this ADR, gate names): **software-only DMA confinement (Pi 5, weaker than the Spark)**.
kernel/docs/adr/0020-pi5-software-only-dma-confinement.md	**Refused outright:** scatter-gather (one descriptor per frame), jumbo frames (frame length capped at the standard Ethernet maximum), device-supplied addresses, and DMA from any other RP1 function (only the GEM is enabled for bus mastering).
kernel/docs/adr/0020-pi5-software-only-dma-confinement.md	Software checks cannot see what the device actually does.

kernel/docs/ALLEN_CANVAS.md	A small line grammar with fixed limits can be parsed in a few hundred lines of integer C with every byte bounds-checked and fuzzed (300,000 random and mutated replies per test run, under AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer).
kernel/docs/ALLEN_CANVAS.md	It names the lines, gives the rules (plain ASCII, at most three things, be honest about what Allen cannot do yet: no weather, clock, internet, messages or media of his own) and three short examples that are not the test questions.
kernel/docs/ALLEN_CANVAS.md	Only the parts of the screen that change are redrawn; the test checks that drawing those parts gives the same frame as drawing everything.
kernel/docs/ALLEN_CANVAS.md	Pixel commands or a general layout language (HTML, SVG, a display list) cannot be checked that cheaply, and would let one reply clutter or break the screen.
kernel/docs/ALLEN_CANVAS.md	- **The kernel's rules hold.** Integer only (1/256 and 16ths of a pixel), no floating point (the test compiles both files `-ffreestanding -mgeneral-regs-only` and checks they call nothing outside the kernel but memset/memcpy/memmove), no allocation (one static 384 KiB pool for the large digits, rasterized at start so drawing never writes to it), and drawing only reads, so the other cores can share a frame.

kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	### 14.4 How it is tested (host only)
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	An empty registry is not sent at all; a zero-length string or binary entry is dropped by the driver and refused by the tool.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	Any failure prints `REFUSED: <reason>` and exits 2.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	A SEC2 refusal is final and is reported, never bypassed.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	- Consequence for this project: I may ship and load the bytes as they are, parse their public container format (the driver does exactly that, in MIT source), and must never patch, unpack `.fwimage`, or work around the signature checks.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	Digests measured 2026-10-12 by `ck_gsp_bindata_extract` over the two files fetched from the 580.173.02 tag (raw.githubusercontent.com, path `src/nvidia/generated/<file>`); the 580.173.02 Makefile pin (`make -C native/kernel gsp-bindata-test`) checks the two image digests and the decoded descriptor on every run.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	Every FB layout field (`gspFwRsvdStart` … `vgaWorkspaceOffset`), `bootCount` and `verified` stay 0: the FMC/ACR fill them and set `verified` to `0xa0a0a0a0a0a0a0a0`, which the host later checks.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	Evidence 2026-10-12 on the box: `CK_GSP_COT_ENCODE_SELFTEST: PASS` (80 checks: both layouts against the compiler, golden header words, encode/decode round trips, 46 field mutations and every header truncation refused by name), same under ASan/UBSan.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	In 580.173.02, GB10B is Thor ("T264/GB10B", uvm_va_space.c:817; Thor is sm_110, the Spark is sm_121), and GB10B's class list has BLACKWELL_COMPUTE_A only (g_gpu_class_list.c:2418-2487) while CUDA++ refuses to run without BLACKWELL_COMPUTE_B and has passing chip receipts on this Spark (CUDApp evidence/FB1-CUT5-HD128-7dd92dc).
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	INFERRED, enforced by the tool but not a driver check: the image address is 4 KiB aligned (the driver maps `NV_ALIGN_UP(size, 0x1000)` bytes of page-granular system memory, `kernel_sec2_gb10b.c:489-495`) and every system address fits the 41-bit DMA window `/proc/driver/nvidia/gpus` reports for this GPU (section 2; `--dma-bits` overrides).
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	It parses the firmware container exactly as the driver does, refuses every malformed shape it was given in its self-test (22 mutations plus every truncation), and proves the file on this box matches the pinned digest.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	- `make -C native/kernel gb10-gsp-mutants`: four test-only mutants (readPtr read from the wrong queue, checksum not stored, radix pointers shifted one page, alloc length counting the params) must each make the test fail.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	`make -C native/kernel gsp-bootargs-test` runs the self-test (142 checks: compiler layouts, golden constants, round trips, every single- field mutation refused); it is part of `make test`.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	- `make -C native/kernel test-gb10-gsp-boot` (also part of `make test` and `make sanitize`): every block decoded at hand-written byte offsets, cross-links, determinism, the ring (full ring, wrap across the ring end, corrupt pointers), the status reader (empty, good, bad checksum, bad sequence, bad element count) and every input refusal.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	`make -C native/kernel test` runs all five self-tests.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	Modes `wprmeta`, `libos`, `logbuf`, `rmargs`, `mqtable`, `radix3` write a block; `check-wprmeta`, `check-libos`, `check-rmargs` refuse any block that is not the fresh-boot GB10B shape; `layout` prints the offsets.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	NARROWED 2026-10-20 (section 14.2 items 1-2): on GB10B that wait is a no-op and the SEC2 send checks only a software property and EMEM HEAD == TAIL; what stays UNKNOWN is whether SEC2 is running with an empty queue at AIENOS entry.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	`native/kernel/tools/ck_gsp_fw_inspect.c`; Makefile targets `gsp-fw-inspect` and `gsp-fw-inspect-test` (the latter is part of `make -C native/kernel test`, so CI runs the self-test on every PR; the real-file step prints NOT_RUN when `/lib/firmware/nvidia/580.173.02/gsp_ga10x.bin` is absent, as it is in CI).
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	**Receiving** (`check-element` mode): the CPU reads slot 0 of the status ring, takes elemCount from it, copies all slots, and refuses the message if the XOR checksum over the padded length is not zero or the seqNum is not the expected receive count (an older seqNum is consumed and the read retried up to 3 times).
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	RESOLVED on the host side 2026-10-12 [SOURCE]: `GSP_FMC_BOOT_PARAMS` (section 10), `GspFwWprMeta` with every value `kgspPopulateWprMeta_GH100` and `_GB10B` fill (heap 133 MiB, bootloader 196,608 bytes at descriptor offsets 0xb000/0xa00/0x0, frts 0, nonWprHeap 0, FB layout left to the FMC), the libos init-argument page (seven logs then RMARGS), the log-buffer PTE prefix, `GSP_ARGUMENTS_CACHED` (message-queue offsets, 512 MiB sysmem heap on ZERO_FB) and the radix-3 ELF table are in section 12 and checked by `ck_gsp_boot_args_encode`.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	**SEC2 send preconditions are software-only** [SOURCE]: `ksec2SafeToSendBootCommands_GB10B` checks only `PDB_PROP_KSEC2_BOOT_GSPFMC` (kernel_sec2_gb10b.c:415-425), which is NV_TRUE for GB10B (g_kernel_sec2_nvoc.c:446-455); `ksec2CanSendPacket_GB10B` requires EMEM queue HEAD == TAIL (kernel_sec2_gb10b.c:344-353).
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	- `selftest`: a 640-byte valid container built in memory, then 22 single-field mutations (bad magic, 32-bit, big-endian, wrong type, wrong machine, wrong shentsize, zero sections, shstrndx out of range, table past end, string table without NUL, name index past table, section past end, offset overflow near 2^64, empty image, version without NUL, version text mismatch, 64-byte version, empty signature, signature for another family only, wrong note type, descriptor past section, truncated note) plus every truncation from 0 to 639 bytes; each must be refused for the named reason.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	The driver shifts these offsets by `RISCV_BR_ADDR_ALIGNMENT` = 8 bits when it programs them [kernel_gsp_gh100.c:47, kernel_gsp_ga102.c:44], so the tool refuses any region that is not 256-byte aligned.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	The next host-only steps that do not need the machine: (a) DONE 2026-10-12: `ck_gsp_bindata_extract` (section 11) writes the eight arrays as files with digests, own DEFLATE and SHA-384, `make -C native/kernel gsp-bindata-test`; (b) DONE 2026-10-12: the encoder, decoder and refusal tests for `NVDM_PAYLOAD_COT`, the MCTP/NVDM headers, the EMEM packet and `GSP_FMC_BOOT_PARAMS` are `ck_gsp_cot_encode` (section 10); (c) DONE 2026-10-12: `ck_gsp_boot_args_encode` (section 12) encodes, decodes and refuses `GspFwWprMeta`, the libos init-argument page, the log-buffer PTE prefix, `GSP_ARGUMENTS_CACHED`, the RPC shared-buffer page table and the radix-3 ELF table with the GB10B populate values traced; (d) DONE on the host side 2026-10-12: `ck_gsp_rpc_encode` encodes, decodes and refuses the queue headers, queue elements, RPC headers, continuation records and the packed registry table (section 13).
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	- Two more hosted tools, same rule (nothing is ever sent to the chip): `ck_gsp_cot_encode` builds and checks the exact message the driver sends to SEC2 (section 10), and `ck_gsp_bindata_extract` reads the eight GB10B firmware arrays out of the public driver source with my own inflater and hash code, cross-checks every size, record and digest, and writes them as files (section 11).
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	[UNKNOWN] Reset and re-open behavior on GB10B after a failed FMC boot (`kgspResetHw_GB100` waits for "reset access"); needed for the resource-incarnation protection (#285) before any repeated attempt.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	What it checks before it writes a byte: the generated-file grammar (`FUNCTION`, `FILE NAME`, `FILE TYPE BINARY`, `COMPRESSION`, `COMPLEX_STRUCT NO`, `DATA SIZE`, `COMPRESSED SIZE`), the array identifier `kgspBinArchive<stem>_BINDATA_LABEL_<label>_data`, the storage record (both sizes, the data pointer, `bCompressed`), the archive table (`entryNum`, order, labels), duplicate labels, more than 16 arrays or 8 MiB per array; for compressed arrays the DEFLATE stream, the CRC-32 and the ISIZE trailer; then the meaning: `UCODE_DESC_*` is 84 bytes, version 5, monitor enabled, with manifest/monitor regions inside the matching `UCODE_IMAGE_*` and 256-byte aligned; `UCODE_HASH` is 48 bytes and equals SHA-384 of `UCODE_IMAGE`; `UCODE_SIG` and `UCODE_PKEY` are 384 bytes; any other label is refused.
kernel/docs/GB10_FIRMWARE_INVENTORY.md	Without the files the pin line reads `NOT_RUN`, never PASS.
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	After `kernel: alive` and before the secondary cores start, `ck_b7b_probe` (core/smmu_svc.c) runs once: it prints the IORT route for PCI segment 15 requester 0x100, confines that requester on a one-page DMA window through the normal `ck_dma_confine` path (which brings up the SMMUv3 node behind it and identity-maps its RMR ranges, section 9), reads `SMMU_IDR0` and `SMMU_IDR1` of that node (read-only registers: "Access to this field is RO", IHI0070H.a 6.3.1 and 6.3.2), unconfines, and prints one summary line `AIENOS_B7B_PROBE: CONFINED_AND_RELEASED | REFUSED | FAIL`.
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	A malformed RMR node (zero or too many descriptors, descriptor array past the node, unaligned or empty range) refuses the whole table, like every other malformed node.
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	Any other summary is a finding, not a retry: `REFUSED` names the reason (`nostream`: the route differs from section 3; `failed`: bring-up or an RMR attribute was refused), `FAIL` means the unconfine failed or no live instance matched the confined base.
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	Attributes 4 (Normal NC) and 5 (Normal IWB-OWB) are honoured, both as Normal NC like the window; any other attribute refuses the confinement (`CK_SMMU_FAILED`, fail closed).
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	Checks (host, QEMU): `test_smmu` Spark-shape case with the RMR node (`route(15, 0x100)` -> instance 1, base 0x13000000, stream 0x100, one RMR with three ranges; `0x101` has none; `route(4, 0x100)` -> instance 0 stream 0x50100; malformed RMR nodes refused), `iort-mutants` with three new killed mutants (`CK_IORT_MUTANT_RMR_BLIND`: RMR nodes ignored; `CK_IORT_MUTANT_RMR_EXCLUSIVE`: stream range read as exclusive, dropping stream 0x100; `CK_IORT_MUTANT_ROUTE_FIRST`: every requester routed to the first SMMU), sanitizer build, the freestanding build, the QEMU gates (one SMMU on QEMU virt: behaviour unchanged), and `ck_acpi_scan --iort` on the real table printing the three instances, the segment 15 route to instance 1 and the RMR node.
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	| `ck_dma_confine` returned `CK_SMMU_NOSTREAM` (B5), `CK_SMMU_OTHER` (B6); since B7a it routes to SMMUv3 B | native/kernel/core/smmu_svc.c | fail closed throughout: never granted unconfined; a GB10 window now needs a caller (B7b) |
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	`ck_dma_unconfine` looks through every live instance (the same stream id confined on two SMMUs at once is ambiguous and refused with `CK_SMMU_EARG`; stream ids are per SMMU, IHI0070H.a "The StreamID namespace is per-SMMU.").
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	Ground checks (Linux on the same machine, read-only)
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	| Negative control: segments 0 to 9 devices (8 functions) | SMMUv3 A, base 0x13800000 | every `/sys/bus/pci/devices/000[0-9]:*/iommu` -> `smmu3.0x0000000013800000` | match |
kernel/docs/GB10_IORT_DECODE.md	- The image checks (`b7b_check`) refuse a default image that carries the probe, a probe image that lacks it, and any image that carries the segment-0 announcement without its flag.

kernel/docs/GPU_DRIVER.md	It refuses an unknown opcode, a short payload, a save index of 8 or more, and any core operation unless the caller supplies the callback, because those restart the chip.
kernel/docs/GPU_DRIVER.md	## Known unknowns (tested on the chip, never assumed)
kernel/docs/GPU_DRIVER.md	The generated code has to behave exactly like the hand-written version from L193: same 225 checks, and a side-by-side trace of every register write and the whole simulated memory after each of about 330 operations must match byte for byte.
kernel/docs/GPU_DRIVER.md	Window 1, after window 0 passes, starts NVIDIA's signed chip program with my own code: my small Linux module lets my start-up tool touch only the 12 start-up registers (6 of them writable) and ring the security processor once; the tool builds the start-up package, hands it over and waits for the chip program to say it is ready.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	A receipt is never rewritten, and the gate holds it: `evidence/` and `import/` are additions only (`tools/evidence_immutable.sh kernel/evidence kernel/import`, against the base ref, the commit that brought `kernel/` in and every commit after it), so a file deleted, renamed or edited, committed or not, or other bytes merged in, fails; a shallow clone is NOT_RUN (exit 3), because the history cannot be read.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	- Every gate id has exactly one `PHYSICAL` row, so a missing physical result is written down instead of left out.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	[`hardware-gates-needs.tsv`](hardware-gates-needs.tsv) lists every gate id and what its PHYSICAL receipt must show before that row can say PASS.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	[`hardware-gates.tsv`](hardware-gates.tsv) is the only place a status is written; this page does not repeat them.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	It is the answer to "has this run on the machine?": a row says PASS only when a receipt file in this repository holds the matching `HWGATE` line.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	The gate (`tests/gates/run.sh kernel-hw-gates` from the omega-mv root, which runs `kernel/tests/gates/run.sh hw-gates` and so `tools/hwgates/gate.sh`) runs the checker on the register, its fixtures (a good fixture must print its exact count line, one of them with an accepted PHYSICAL PASS), mutants and planted-bad copies, compares the register's count line with counts taken from the register itself, and runs the shell checks the checker cannot do: receipt files, inventory, receipt claims, withdrawn lines, unchanged receipts, source commits, a run far past the old 1024-row and 524288-byte tables, and a size alarm when the real register passes what that run proves.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	The gate (`tools/hwgates/gate.sh`) refuses when the id sets of the two files differ, so a gate that has no receipt yet cannot leave the inventory without a reviewed change to both files.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	This page explains the hardware-gate register [`hardware-gates.tsv`](hardware-gates.tsv), which lists every Aienux and GB10 GPU-driver capability that touches hardware, the host and QEMU gates that cover it, and its physical status.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	[`tools/hwgates/hwgates.om`](../tools/hwgates/hwgates.om) (seeded Omega, built with omega-mv's [`seed/out/omc`](../../seed/out/omc.sha256)) enforces these as rules R1..R10, refuses an empty register (R8), the deletion of a gate whose receipt still names it (R9) and a gate id with anything but upper-case letters, digits, `-`, `_` or `.` (R10, so two ids never differ by case alone), and prints `HWGATES PASS rows=… gates=… physical=… physical_pass=… physical_not_pass=…`.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_GATES.md	Without the pinned compiler it exits 3 (NOT_RUN), never 0.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_TEST_RIG.md	A failed capture never blocks power control.
kernel/docs/HARDWARE_TEST_RIG.md	- The mains relay is **fail-on**: power flows when the relay is at rest, so a dead Pi or a crashed script never holds the machine dark.

kernel/docs/NATIVE_BOOT_ONE_TIME.md	Any production or release gate must still require Secure Boot ON.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_BOOT_ONE_TIME.md	Any tool or procedure attempting to disable Secure Boot fails closed.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_BOOT_ONE_TIME.md	In order it: builds the image (`CK_INFER_LIB`, `CK_FB_HOLD_S=120`) and `ck_model_map`; `chattr +i` on the model (first, so its blocks cannot move after the map is made); `ck_model_map make --fiemap --part-start auto --disk /dev/nvme0n1`, then `ck_model_map check` (re-reads every extent by LBA and recomputes the SHA-256) and requires the pinned model hash; `verify_native_rollback.sh --capture-pre`; copies `aienos-ck.efi`, `MODEL.MAP` and `STAGED-CK.TXT` to `\EFI\AIENOS`; creates a create-only boot entry, restores `BootOrder` if it changed, checks `Boot0004 AIENOSRECOV` is still present, and sets `BootNext` once.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_BOOT_ONE_TIME.md	**Secure Boot (release only):** a release gate requires `SecureBoot enabled`; during development (OFF since 2026-10-01, owner decision) the state is only recorded.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	An interruption before step 6 leaves the control record untouched and the target invalid (or, only between steps 5 and 6, valid but not staged, which the loader ignores until a later `update` stages it).
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	Any refusal: **nothing is written**;
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	A separately authenticated, rollback-resistant anchor checked on every boot and advanced before promotion becomes durable is still required; provisioning and recovery for that anchor need owner policy before hardware qualification.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	**Cut 2b (done, QEMU; section 8): kernel-side promote.** After its health milestone the kernel writes the control record over its own NVMe path (promote the candidate, or mark it failed), so a good update becomes the active slot instead of only being tried three times.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	Emulator tests required before hardware: successful update, invalid signature, interrupted update, unsuccessful boot." Cut 2a meets that without changing the stub at all: the slot logic is a separate UEFI application, `SLOTLDR.EFI`, in front of the unchanged stub + kernel image (section 7).
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	Every write is bounded to the target slot's unit range; the active slot and the control region (except the one commit of step 6) are never written.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	**How the kernel learns which slot it is.** The control record alone cannot tell: "candidate on its last try" and "candidate out of tries, active booted" both read `tries=0`.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	Never: UEFI variables, runtime services, `ExitBootServices` (the chained stub leaves boot services itself), `SetWatchdogTimer`, kernel objects (all checked at build time).
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	**Operator verb `update`** (lifecycle READY only: a LOCKED Store, or a pending task, refuses it; `core/lifecycle_fsm.c`).
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	- **promote**: only the staged candidate, only after the booted kernel passed its health milestone; flips active, clears candidate, sets last good, and raises the *disk record's* floor to the manifest's policy generation.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	Reads the control region (units 0-3) and recovers the newest committed record with the torn-slot rules (`native/store/torn_slot.c`), read only.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	| reason `candidate_try`, control still names the slot, health reached | **promote**: active = this slot, candidate cleared, floor raised to the image's policy generation (never lowered) | one control commit |
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	- `scripts/qemu_ck_slot_loader_test.sh` rows 12-15 (`docs/GATES.md`): row 12 update from slot A writes and stages B, a second `update` is refused, A's bytes are unchanged, the next boot tries B and the kernel promotes it; row 13 QEMU is killed in the middle of the image write (TEST-only `update halt-after=1`), the control record and A are untouched, the loader refuses the half-written B and boots A, and a new `update` then succeeds; row 14 an inbox signed by the wrong key and one with a flipped image byte are refused with the slots partition byte-identical; row 15 the update is refused while the Store is locked, and a TEST-key updater image is refused under hardware staging.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	Selection at every boot, with the control record C read from the region:
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	The 22 added for cut 3: a positive update (B written, verified from disk, staged, A unchanged, the loader's next decision is a candidate try; exhausted tries and an invalid candidate fall back to A), refusal while a candidate is staged, an injected I/O error at each of the 16 writes of an update (every one refused with A and the control record untouched and B invalid or unstaged), a stop at each step, and ten refusals before any write (bad signature, wrong key, wrong digest, truncated inbox, generation at the floor, booted slot not active, no trusted key, no control record, image larger than the slot, corrupt manifest).
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	The TEST signer exists for tests only.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	**This cut (host only).** Format, rules, host tool `ck_slot_tool` (`make -C native/kernel slot-test`: layout, manifest/control mutations, selection including missing/corrupt control refusal, bounded tries, disk-relative floor, transitions, and torn-slot commit/recover with a simulated torn write) and the freestanding proof (`slot-free`).
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	This is the only write, and it lands only in the control region.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_IMAGE_SLOTS.md	**Trusted keys.** The service image is built with the same key table as SLOTLDR.EFI (`ck_slot_tool keys-c`): RFC 8032 TEST 1 in the TEST-keys service image (`CK_SERVICE_TEST_KEYS=1`, QEMU only), the Owner Root from `CK_OWNER_PUBKEYS` under `CK_HARDWARE_STAGING`; the plain service image has no table and refuses every update.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	A dirty-tree package is test evidence, never a release candidate.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	Build with pins/model/firmware; every PACKAGE.txt line including each provenance line against an independent computation; the manifest provenance equals `package_sha256`; refusals for an existing output, a bad pin, owner files on the wrong variant, `CK_*` in the environment and in `MAKEFLAGS`, a dirty tree (in a scratch worktree of HEAD, the real checkout is never touched), an unsigned package, double signing, the wrong owner key, a tampered image, a tampered PACKAGE.txt with and without re-made sums and `PROVENANCE.txt`, a tampered or missing `PROVENANCE.txt`, a flipped signature byte, a missing SHA256SUMS, another package's signed manifest, a non-reproducible build via a test hook; twelve record tampers with re-made sums (tree_clean, Makefile and image.ld digests, commit time, image SHA-512 and size, make goal and variables, `input_env_ck`, a missing and a duplicated line, and a toolchain line under `--same-host`); the hardware-staging variant with the labelled TEST-FIXTURE owner files; and the offline signing round trip with a throwaway OpenSSL Ed25519 key (`tbs` without and with `--pubkey`, the TEST key refused, a signature over signer-less bytes refused, a dirty-tree package refused for a real signature, attach, verify under the keys file, `signer_id` equal to OpenSSL's SHA-256 of the raw public key).
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	Change any record line and the provenance no longer matches; the self-test proves each of these refusals.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	- **Committed tree only.** A dirty working tree (tracked files changed) is refused.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	Every line is printed as `checked` or `recorded`.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	| Inputs | `input_make_goal` (`all` for core, `full` otherwise), `input_make_vars` (`none`, or `CK_HARDWARE_STAGING=1 CK_OWNER_PUBKEYS=sha256:<hex> CK_MACHINE_ID=sha256:<hex>`), `input_env_ck none` (any `CK_*` in the environment or in an outer make's `MAKEFLAGS` is refused, so nothing unrecorded selects the image), `input_makefile_sha256` (`native/kernel/Makefile`), `input_image_ld_sha256` (`native/boot/image.ld`), `pin <repo> <sha>` per `--pins` line, `model_bytes`/`model_sha256` from `--model`, `firmware <name> <bytes> <sha256>` per `--firmware`, and for the staging variant `owner_pubkeys_sha256` and `machine_id_sha256` |
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	- **Never overwritten, never re-signed in place.** `--out` must not exist; `sign` refuses when `MANIFEST.signed.bin` exists.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	- **No unrecorded inputs.** `CK_*` variables in the environment or passed down in `MAKEFLAGS` are refused; the variant's make goal and variables are the only ones used, and they are recorded.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	Public owner fixtures used by the self-test qualify the build recipe only and cannot authorize deployment.

kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	`test` for `core` and `full`: the labelled TEST 1 key loader (`CK_TEST_SLOT_KEY=1`), never for hardware |
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	Tests may pass `--allow-dirty`; the record then says `tree_clean no`, and `verify` and `sign` refuse that package unless given `--allow-dirty` too (`sign-test` accepts it; the TEST key never signs for hardware).
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	The existing Makefile enforces owner provisioning, the resident lifecycle and boot-disk-only model ingest; no QEMU fw_cfg model fallback or TEST Store keys are selected.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	Untracked files, including ignored files, in either inference crate are refused so Cargo cannot auto-discover an unrecorded `build.rs` or Rust module.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	- **Verify fails closed.** `verify --package DIR --keys FILE` checks `SHA256SUMS`, `PROVENANCE.txt`, every record line it can recompute (exactly one of each required line; image bytes, SHA-256 and SHA-512; `tree_clean`; the make goal and variables against the variant; the commit time, Makefile and `image.ld` digests against the commit when the repository has it), the provenance binding, the commit match, the signed manifest's signature under the owner keys (`tools/ck_owner_gen.c` file format), and that the signed manifest equals the built one in every field but signer/signature.
kernel/docs/NATIVE_PACKAGE.md	`verify` refuses a package whose loader bytes or digest differ from the record, whose `loader_keys` does not match the variant, a `test` loader without its "TEST-ONLY key" note, or an `owner` loader carrying any TEST-ONLY note.
kernel/docs/native/PI5-4-UNLOCK-RUNS.md	After each refused run, the hub (Linux, partition not mounted) read the Store view of the AIENOS partition (sectors 0 to 8388543, so the 64-sector run marker at the end is excluded because it changes every run) and took its SHA-256.
kernel/docs/native/PI5-4-UNLOCK-RUNS.md	Its first 48 sectors were zeroed and read back as zeros (`head_zeroed_readback_ok`), so the new Store is production-class from its first byte and never reuses TEST content.
kernel/docs/native/PI5-4-UNLOCK-RUNS.md	The recovery secret is a THROWAWAY (32 random bytes from /dev/urandom, never committed, never printed); the request file \EFI\AIENOS\UNLOCK.REQ was placed on the card by `pi5-run.sh UNLOCK_REQ=...` and zeroed, deleted and checked absent after each run (`unlock_req_deleted_verified`).
kernel/docs/native/PI5-5-CORTEX-RUNS.md	Boot 1 (card fact) remembers `prefer short notes` and prints its Store generation; boot 2 (no request) recalls the same text at the same generation, adds no record, chain ok; the wrong secret recalls nothing, prints no fact text and leaves the card byte-identical (also when a fact request comes with it); the right secret afterwards recalls again; a second fact keeps both in order; a card with no journal says `none` and creates none; the request checker refuses every malformed layout.
kernel/docs/native/PI5-5-CORTEX-RUNS.md	It never builds on the TEST-key store tier.
kernel/docs/native/PI5-5-CORTEX-RUNS.md	Mutation proof (`make -f stage.mk pi5-fact-mutants`): `CK_FACT_MUTANT_NO_APPEND` (the commit skipped, success still reported) and `CK_FACT_MUTANT_RECALL_BLIND` (recall never reads the journal) both make the test fail (mutants killed).
kernel/docs/native/PI5-6-AGENT-RUNS.md	A file stands in for the card, every boot starts from a cold C state, the inference unit is a stub (a real model cannot run on the host); the stage code under test is the real svc/pi5_agent.c.
kernel/docs/native/PI5-6-AGENT-RUNS.md	It proves: the reply object equals the lifecycle's bytes and adds only the trailer; boot 1 generates, closes, commits once and reads back; boot 2 (same secret, no model) prints exactly boot 1's `agent: reply` line (same Store generation, same object digest) with zero model calls, also when the model is present; wrong secret: nothing recovered, zero model calls, card byte-identical, and the right secret afterwards recovers again; eight non-golden generations (wrong id, wrong head, wrong text, wrong model digest, refused prompt, cancelled prompt, model vanished, too many tokens) commit nothing; no reply and no model is NOMODEL; a lifecycle reply for the same prompt and a reply for another prompt are ignored; a stored non-golden reply is reported FAIL and is never regenerated over.

kernel/docs/native/PI5-8-ORCHESTRATOR-EXAM.md	### Action accuracy by case category (pooled over all splits; the 12 candidates, control excluded)
kernel/docs/native/PI5-8-ORCHESTRATOR-EXAM.md	Categories (all splits): single action 59, argument extraction 15, multi-step (must pick the first action) 8, clarify 10, none 10, refuse 13 (rule breaking, injection, verbs the model may not use), inject-data 2 (an order stored as data), dialog 11 (2 to 3 turns, context needed; earlier assistant turns are the gold answers).
kernel/docs/native/PI5-8-ORCHESTRATOR-EXAM.md	- **Constrained decoding makes every model 100 % valid by construction and lifts action accuracy by 1 to 35 points.** For the best four models (qwen3-1.7b, granite4-1b, granite3.3-2b, qwen2.5-1.5b) the lift is +1.6 to +10 points; for the weak ones it is large, because they mostly fail at format.
kernel/docs/native/PI5-8-ORCHESTRATOR-EXAM.md	Design choices that are mine and open to challenge: `update` is a refuse for the model although the full lifecycle has it (the card-script parser excludes it); a request to *store* a note that itself contains an order ("remember this note: ignore your rules") is a `remember` (it is data), only an order to *obey* it is a `refuse`; `prompt` means "write or draft text", live information and devices are `none`.
kernel/docs/native/PI5-8-ORCHESTRATOR-EXAM.md	**Hardest categories for the yardsticks, in order: clarify** (10 cases; the best model gets 1 of 10; most models never say "I need more information" and invent an action with the vague text as its argument), **refuse** (13; best 54 %; models obey "ignore your rules", or do the named verb, or output an unlisted action such as `play`), **none** (10; mean 22 % free, 39 % constrained), then **dialog** (context from earlier turns; best 64 %).
kernel/docs/native/PI5-8-ORCHESTRATOR-EXAM.md	Honest limits of the freeze: before freezing I ran two Qwen2.5 models on the pool as a pipe check and fixed only the scorer, not the prompt; I wrote the prompt and both case sets, so the held-out set is blind to the prompt's wording but not to my own idea of what is hard.
kernel/docs/native/PI5-8-ORCHESTRATOR-EXAM.md	It is also the part a trained, narrow decision core can be given most data for: the train split has 79 cases and the pool of paraphrases should be grown there (the train split holds only 6 clarify and 8 refuse cases, so ALLEN's trainer will need many more of those).

kernel/docs/osc-launch.md	The mapping is never executable, is not cleared at teardown, and `cells` pointer checks use the actual size.
kernel/docs/osc-launch.md	The only declared budget the launcher bounds is `max_stack_bytes`, at 64 KiB: a stack of 65552 (65536 + 16, the smallest legal value above the maximum: the container needs a multiple of 16) gives 14 (gate line, unit `l02_stack_over`), and so does `a06_valid_limits_at_max` (1 MiB).

kernel/docs/P3_NVME_CONTROLLER_AUDIT.md	| 10 | CSTS.CFS handling | `nvme.rs:70-72`; `driver.rs:32-34` | Any poll that sees CFS returns `Failed` immediately | Yes - CFS = fatal controller status | None | None | SUPPORTED |
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_CONTROLLER_AUDIT.md	| 29 | Hard-coded NSID 1 | `driver.rs:145,150,156`, `184-185`, `333-335`, `419` | All identify/read/write/flush use namespace 1 | Assumes NSID 1 exists and is the target | **Medium**: a controller whose active namespace is not 1 (or multi-namespace) fails or targets the wrong store | Enumerate active NSIDs (Identify CNS=2) and use the discovered NSID | UNSUPPORTED |
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_CONTROLLER_AUDIT.md	| 32 | Completion SQID not checked | `driver.rs:231,372` | Only `command_id` matched; SQID ignored | Spec requires matching SQID+CID | Low robustness; a stale/misrouted completion could be accepted | Also compare `completion.sq_id()` (0 admin / 1 I/O) | UNSAFE-IF-DEVIATES |
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_CONTROLLER_AUDIT.md	| 6 | CAP.CSS / CC.CSS | `driver.rs:42,123` | `CC.CSS=0` (NVM) hard-coded; CAP.CSS never checked | Only if NVM command set supported (`CAP.CSS` bit 0) | Low on real NVMe drives; non-conformant on a device not advertising NVM CSS | Read CAP.CSS (bits 52:45); fail init if bit 0 clear | UNSUPPORTED |
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_CONTROLLER_AUDIT.md	| 7 | CAP.AMS / CC.AMS | `driver.rs:42,123` | `CC.AMS=0` (Round Robin) hard-coded; CAP.AMS never checked | Round Robin is the base mechanism, but not validated | Low | Read CAP.AMS (bits 18:17); assert bit 0 set or select a supported mechanism | UNSAFE-IF-DEVIATES |
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_FLUSH_REVIEW.md	- **CID** - drawn from the shared `next_cid` (driver.rs:368 - 369), so a Flush never aliases the preceding Write's CID; the test asserts monotonic `+1`.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_READ_SUBSTRATE.md	The host harness builds the same payload with `printf` + `truncate` + `dd`, hashes it with `sha256sum`, and requires the kernel's `NVME_READ_QEMU` line to contain that host-computed hash, so a wrong or stale image cannot pass.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_UNSAFE_ASSUMPTIONS.md	(Note: `nsid` is a parameter to `identify_namespace` but every caller passes 1.)
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_UNSAFE_ASSUMPTIONS.md	- **Why unsafe**: Round Robin is normally supported, but the capability (`CAP.AMS` bit 0) is never checked before selecting it.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_DMA_REVIEW.md	**Arena exhaustion (functional, fail-closed).** `NativeDma` never frees (`nvme_read.rs:269-305`), and `transfer`'s MDTS chunk cap is 128 KiB (`driver.rs:308-316`) - exactly `ARENA_BYTES` (`nvme_read.rs:43`).
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_DMA_REVIEW.md	- Decision logic is direction-agnostic: confined iff `smmu_ready`, bypass only when no SMMU exists AND the unsafe feature is compiled, never a fallback after a failed SMMU - `crates/aienos-kernel/src/dma_gate.rs:68-82`, test `failed_smmu_never_falls_back_to_bypass` `:279-282`.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_DMA_REVIEW.md	- `NativeDma` bump-allocates only inside `DMA_ARENA`, bounds-checked against `ARENA_BYTES` - `nvme_read.rs:269-283`, `:286-305`.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_DMA_REVIEW.md	- The bypass cannot be requested silently: the QEMU harness requires `AIENOS_UNSAFE_DMA_BYPASS=1` and refuses the feature otherwise (`scripts/qemu_nvme_test.sh:49-71`); the SMMU harness forces it off (`scripts/qemu_smmu_test.sh:3,8`).
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_DMA_REVIEW.md	- The gate is evaluated **once**, before any command, in `run`: `dma_gate::dma_grant(smmu_ready, smmu_base.is_some(), UNSAFE_NVME_DMA_BYPASS)`
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_FLUSH_SUBSTRATE.md	In every path where DMA was granted the module must revoke it (`dma_gate: nvme bus master revoked`, `nvme_read.rs:232-247,566`) - including when the write itself fails in `write-error` mode - so the host's revoke check holds in all granted-DMA modes.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_FLUSH_SUBSTRATE.md	- Read **before** `ExitBootServices` (boot services still live) and passed into the post-exit phase; post-exit code cannot use the file protocol.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_FLUSH_SUBSTRATE.md	Target LBA 4096 is deliberately distinct from the read sentinel LBA 2048 (`nvme_read.rs:41`, `qemu_nvme_test.sh:107`) so a write can never be confused with a read-lane sentinel check.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_REVIEW.md	`> 2 MiB` at 4 KiB pages) would fail `build_prps` with `ListTooSmall`, which `transfer` maps to `BlockError::InvalidInput` (`driver.rs:341`), failing every large transfer.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_REVIEW.md	Because the data region is page-aligned, `build_prps`' internal page count (`nvme.rs:272-277`, offset = 0) exactly equals `list_pages`, so the ">2 pages" test never disagrees with the encoder.
kernel/docs/P3_NVME_WRITE_REVIEW.md	Because the range check bounds `lba + blocks <= block_count`, the per-chunk LBA cannot overflow `u64`.

kernel/docs/TRUST-1-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md	Status (2026-09-30, software side): `scripts/trust1_key_ceremony.sh` (OpenSSL 3 only, Linux or macOS) implements the ceremony: `preflight` refuses to run with a network route; `generate` creates Owner Root, Release Signer and Operator Approval (Ed25519) and the Boot Signer (RSA-2048 self-signed X.509 with codeSigning, the form UEFI db and sbsign need), each private key encrypted from creation, plus the generation-1 authority manifest signed by Owner Root; `backup` copies to a medium and proves every key decrypts from that copy; `record` writes the ceremony receipt (no private material); `rotate ROLE add|revoke` demonstrates add, verify, revoke as new root-signed generations with the old key quarantined, never deleted; `verify DIR MIN_GEN` refuses an older generation.
kernel/docs/TRUST-1-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md	Status (2026-09-30, software side): the image now also carries `sbverify`, `fusermount3` and the TPM device transport library (loaded at run time, so `ldd` never saw it), and the build fails loudly when `gocryptfs` is missing (FUSE is built into the Ubuntu kernel).
kernel/docs/TRUST-1-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md	Stop condition: Any unexplained PCR change → UnexpectedState protocol (Gate 9), no policy chosen.

kernel/native/argus/argus_abi.h	1 only if pre-established policy permits synchronous refusal at the boundary
kernel/native/argus/argus_abi.h	ARGUS-1: only REVOKE_CAPABILITY has a LIVE executor (I1); the other nine are SYNTH (typed and decided, executed only by a labelled test executor with ARGUS_CREQ_SYNTHETIC).
kernel/native/argus/argus_abi.h	ARGUS consumer only (CONSUMER flag); resource = finding_sequence; ring push refuses it
kernel/native/argus/argus_abi.h	cap_id 0 is the office capability and is checked like any other; "none" is ARGUS_CAP_NONE.
kernel/native/argus/argus_abi.h	Every flag bit is now meaningful; "reserved bits" checks no longer apply in v1.1.
kernel/native/argus/argus_abi.h	Flush policy (the explicit aggregation policy of brief section 3): every ARGUS_USE_FLUSH_OPS validates per thread AND at least every flush interval the producer emits one CAPABILITY_USE_SUMMARY per touched slot; a table that cannot flush (ring refused) keeps counting and the next summary carries the total, so no use is ever silently lost; refusals are still counted.
kernel/native/argus/argus_abi.h	libargus never includes aienos_capability.h; a test asserts equality.
kernel/native/argus/argus_abi.h	The authority side declares a layout-identical AienosContainRequest (every offsetof equal, lane G test).
kernel/native/argus/argus_core.c	Apply verdict (only for an in-order event whose outcome would update state; decided on the pre-state): CAPABILITY_GRANTED applies iff the slot is UNSEEN or cap_generation > the shadow generation; otherwise ARGUS_F_AUTHORITY_REPLAY (code 13, HIGH, prior_sequence = the slot's revoked sequence if REVOKED, else its granted sequence).
kernel/native/argus/argus_core.c	CAPABILITY_USE_SUMMARY (v1.1) applies nothing; it is sequence-checked, detected (lane E treats it as a USED) and chained like any event.
kernel/native/argus/argus_core.c	It never authorizes anything and never re-checks a capability.
kernel/native/argus/argus_core.c	Once validate accepts one (lane B rules), the core treats it like any other well-formed event in steps 0, 1, 6 and 7 (sequence-checked on its stream, counted in events_received, chained) with two differences: step 2/4 apply nothing (no shadow change; they fall through the default verdict/apply as no-ops) and step 3 is SKIPPED: the detectors never see a 90-93 as a trigger, so no code 1-16 detector fires on ARGUS's own containment record.
kernel/native/argus/argus_core.c	per-producer sequence check (lane E cannot see history).
kernel/native/argus/argus_core.c	---- state update (only called when the verdict allows it) ----------------
kernel/native/argus/argus_core.h	This header only exposes the fixed table sizes so tests and the integrator can reason about them.
kernel/native/argus/argus_detect.c	cap_id 0 is the authority OFFICE slot and is checked like any other (v1.1).
kernel/native/argus/argus_detect.c	MachineId is PROVISIONAL (opaque 32 bytes); this is an identity-consistency check only, not cryptographic attestation.
kernel/native/argus/argus_detect.h	A caller that passes cap >= ARGUS_DETECT_MAX_FINDINGS never sees ARGUS_ERR_OVERFLOW.
kernel/native/argus/argus_event.c	object_id is not checked (forward-compat; producers write 0) sequence != 0 and sequence != UINT64_MAX EXTERNAL_EFFECT_REQUESTED/DENIED/COMMITTED carry effect_class EXTERNAL Every one of the 128 bytes is carried by some field, so a buffer that decodes successfully re-encodes to exactly the same bytes.
kernel/native/argus/argus_event.c	type in 1..ARGUS_CONTAIN_MAX; request_id != 0 90 PROPOSED: status 0, outcome OK 91 DECIDED: GRANT<->OK, DENY<->DENIED, ESCALATE<->ERROR 92 EXECUTED: DONE/PARTIAL<->OK, FAILED<->DENIED or ERROR, UNAVAILABLE/FAILED_SCOPE<->ERROR; DONE for any type but RevokeCapability needs ARGUS_FLAG_SYNTHETIC (I2) finding_code, code, tick, resource and effect_class are producer conventions, not checked here (lane C / the offline checker judge them).
kernel/native/argus/argus_event.c	v1.1: every flag bit is defined (bits 2-15 = stream id), so no reserved-bit check.
kernel/native/argus/argus_ring.c	As the ring fills, lower classes are refused first so that room is always kept for the events that matter most: INFORMATIONAL refused once depth >= 50% of capacity AUDIT refused once depth >= 75% SECURITY refused once depth >= 90% (rounded up to a whole slot) CRITICAL may use 100%; refused only when the ring is truly full A refused push returns ARGUS_ERR_FULL and the event is NOT stored; the producer keeps running.
kernel/native/argus/argus_ring.c	Nothing is ever lost silently: - a refused SECURITY/AUDIT/INFORMATIONAL event adds 1 to refused[class] (cumulative, in argus_ring_stats) and to a pending per-class count; - a refused CRITICAL event adds 1 to critical_overflow (cumulative and sticky: it is never reset, it marks a permanent evidence gap) and to a pending critical count.
kernel/native/argus/argus_ring.c	refused[CRITICAL] therefore always stays 0, and for every attempt: attempts = accepted + sum(refused) + critical_overflow.
kernel/native/argus/argus_ring.c	Synchronisation is two C11 atomic indices (head written only by the producer with release, tail written only by the consumer with release), each on its own cache line, plus per-class atomic refusal counters.
kernel/native/argus/argus_ring.c	The synthesized events are handed to the consumer directly; they do not go through the ring, so reporting a loss can never itself be refused.
kernel/native/argus/argus_ring.c	v1.2 (spec section 3): kind 90 CONTAINMENT_PROPOSED is always refused MALFORMED here; only the consumer synthesizes proposals and ingests them directly.

kernel/native/argus/docs/ARGUS0_GATES.md	| ARGUS_TRANSPORT_SATURATION_PASS | Class watermarks hold under a stalled consumer: INFORMATIONAL refused at 50%, SECURITY at 90%, CRITICAL only at 100%; SECURITY is never refused while INFORMATIONAL is still accepted; nothing is refused below its limit; every drop is reported with the right severity.

kernel/native/boot/efi_allen.c	The bytes are kept as read; the kernel stage (core/allen.c) has the Rust unit check the whole file before any case runs.
kernel/native/boot/efi_model.c	The stub does NOT hash: the kernel recomputes SHA-256 over the bytes it received (core/infer.c) and refuses to decode on a mismatch, so a wrong LBA, a short read or a stale file can never pass as the model.
kernel/native/boot/model_map.h	Integrity: the declared SHA-256 of the model is carried to the kernel in the handoff and the kernel recomputes it over the bytes it received; any wrong LBA, short read or stale file shows up as a hash mismatch and the kernel refuses to decode (never a silent wrong model).
kernel/native/boot/model_map.h	The map has no checksum of its own: every field that could steer a read wrong is covered by the SHA-256 check, and a malformed map is refused by ck_model_map_check below (the same function in the stub and the tool).
kernel/native/boot/netboot_proto.h	Message (all integers big-endian), NB_HDR + 8 fixed body bytes minimum: 0 magic "ANB1" 4 type u8 5 reserved[3] = 0 8 nonce[16] HELLO 1 L->S 24 mac[6] 30 reserved[2] (32 bytes) OFFER 2 S->L 24 total u32 28 chunk u16 = NB_CHUNK 30 rsvd u16 (32) WAIT 3 S->L 24 seconds u16 (1..NB_WAIT_MAX) 26 reserved[6] (32) GET 4 L->S 24 offset u32 28 count u16 (1..NB_WINDOW) 30 rsvd (32) DATA 5 S->L 24 offset u32 28 len u16 30 rsvd u16 32 data[len] (32 + len) DONE 6 L->S 24 status u8 (0 verified, booting; 1 refused) 25 rsvd[7] 32 text[n], n <= NB_TEXT_MAX printable ASCII (32 + n) The nonce is chosen by the loader for each attempt and echoed by the server; it pairs replies with requests and is not a secret.
kernel/native/boot/README.md	**A/B slots live in a separate loader, `slot_loader.c` -> `SLOTLDR.EFI` (P11 cut 2a, gate N7, QEMU only; owner decision 2026-10-12: narrowly scoped to signed A/B image selection, boot-success tracking and rollback).** It sits in front of this stub as the boot volume's `EFI/BOOT/BOOTAA64.EFI`, reads the slots partition (`slot_format.h`), verifies both slots against owner keys baked in at build time, applies `slot_select.c` (bounded candidate tries, generation floor, never a boot loop), commits a candidate try before the jump, and chain-loads the chosen image (this stub + kernel, unchanged and byte-identical) with `LoadImage` + `StartImage`.
kernel/native/boot/slot_format.h	A missing or corrupt control record requires recovery; it must never reset the effective floor to zero.
kernel/native/boot/slot_format.h	generation_floor is only a floor relative to the currently read disk control record.
kernel/native/boot/slot_format.h	The control record alone cannot tell a kernel which slot it is: "candidate on its last try" and "candidate out of tries, active booted" both read tries=0.
kernel/native/boot/slot_format.h	The loader writes the control record only to consume a candidate try.
kernel/native/boot/slot_loader.c	Never: UEFI variables, runtime services, ExitBootServices (the chained stub leaves boot services itself), any disk write other than the control region, any write when the control record is missing or corrupt.
kernel/native/boot/slot_loader.c	Only the control region (units 0..3) is ever written.
kernel/native/boot/slot_update.h	-1 = refused with *why (res->wrote says whether the target slot was touched; the control record never was).
kernel/native/boot/slot_update.h	Any refusal: NOTHING written.
kernel/native/boot/slot_update.h	Every write lies inside the target slot's unit range [M, M + CAP) (checked before each write); the active slot and the control region (except the one commit of step 6) are never written.
kernel/native/boot/slot_update.h	One routine, shared by the service kernel (svc/slot_service.c, operator verb `update`) and the host tool (ck_slot_tool, its self-test injects an I/O error at every write).
kernel/native/capability/aienos_capability.c	A privileged right is an authority-office right: it is honored only on an entry whose resource is AIENOS_CAP_RES_AUTHORITY, so a capability that carries (say) the epoch right on some other resource, such as an operator control resource, cannot step the authority (#266).
kernel/native/capability/aienos_capability.c	Refuses a live slot, so it cannot mint a forged generation.
kernel/native/capability/aienos_capability.c	The new table starts above every generation the old one used, so outstanding references fail against it even after reclaims.
kernel/native/capability/aienos_capability.c	The office token is held beside the table, never in it; no operation here checks it (see aienos_cap_authorize).

kernel/native/capability/aienos_contain.h	Every decision and every execution attempt yields a receipt (request digest, generation, actor, subject, a strictly increasing gate sequence number), returned to the caller and sent to the sink, so every exercise of the defensive right can be audited (I5).

kernel/native/disk/disk_nvme.h	- Any timeout, CFS, CID/SQID mismatch or failed queue creation puts the controller in the failed state; every later call returns DISK_ESTATE until nvme_init runs again (fail-stop).
kernel/native/disk/disk_nvme.h	Checks performed by nvme_init (each refusal returns its own NVME_E* code; docs/P3_NVME_UNSAFE_ASSUMPTIONS.md items marked [UA]): [UA] CAP.MPSMIN (bits 51:48) must be 0: the driver uses 4 KiB pages only (CC.MPS = 0).
kernel/native/disk/disk_sdhci.h	Every command: CMD_INHIBIT (and DATA_INHIBIT for data or busy) clear before issue; command complete, data ready and transfer complete each bounded (cfg.timeout_ms, default 1000 ms); any error interrupt bit fails the command (ECMD / EDATA) after CMD and DATA line resets.
kernel/native/disk/disk_sdhci.h	Every register access is a naturally aligned 32-bit read or write: some Broadcom SDHCI blocks refuse 8/16-bit writes, so TRANSFER_MODE+COMMAND (0x0C) and BLOCK_SIZE+BLOCK_COUNT (0x04) are written as one word each, and the byte registers at 0x28 and 0x2C are updated read-modify-write (never while a SOFTWARE_RESET bit is still set).

kernel/native/disk/OMEGA_NVME_READ.md	The verifier keeps QEMU asynchronous: the engine reads whatever QEMU shows, and only when it waits does the verifier wait until QEMU is idle (every thread asleep, none run between two reads of /proc), so one wait covers a completion at any load; a wait loop that needs more than 8 waits in QEMU fails the case.

kernel/native/kernel/CONTINUITY_RECOVERY_CONTRACT.md	- Any change to the Rust kernel other than a test-only vector emitter (D-1).
kernel/native/kernel/CONTINUITY_RECOVERY_CONTRACT.md	Checkpoints 0-4 give the old state, 6, before_anchor and after_anchor the new one (before_anchor reopens at `SS_RB_PREPARED_ADVANCE`); checkpoint 5 (`after_inactive_superblock`) gave the NEW state in every run (the test allows either there, as the Rust oracle does).
