kernel/native/kernel/CONTINUITY_RECOVERY_CONTRACT.md	**Cut 5: CONFIRMED for a flipped byte of the newest AgentRoot (see K-3: `SS_E_ROLLBACK`, reason `SealedRefusal(-303)`, no action applicable); cut 7: host test written (`t_k4`), covering only a sealed-valid newer MANIFEST (next sequence, fresh anchor) whose agent_state object was never written, NOT_RUN until the forge reports; predicted outcome: the Store mounts Valid, reason `ContinuityCorrupt("referenced object is absent")` (continuity_recovery.c:102-111, continuity_resolve.c:75), no action applicable.
kernel/native/kernel/CONTINUITY_RECOVERY_CONTRACT.md	- **D-1 Golden vectors.** A test-only Rust emitter (no change to Rust kernel behaviour; the Rust kernel is frozen as reference) writes canonical bytes and ObjectIds for a fixed set of inputs: AgentRoot (both sources), manifests with 0/1/64 WAL ids, AgentState genesis and after forks at depth 1 and 2, WAL segments with each epistemic status and statement lengths 1 and 1024, root/child branch ids (index 0, 1, 2^32, 2^64-1), challenges for both actions, and HMAC responses with the TEST key.

kernel/native/kernel/core/acpi_dev.h	Every read is bounds checked against the table length; the table itself must pass signature, length and checksum checks first.
kernel/native/kernel/core/acpi_dev.h	Returns the number of matching devices (it may exceed max; only max are stored), or a CK_AML_E_* for a table that fails ck_aml_table_ok.
kernel/native/kernel/core/acpi.h	Calls fn(lo, hi, ctx) with the byte span [lo, hi) of the RSDP (its checked length), the XSDT/RSDT and every table the root lists (at least the 36-byte header each, whatever their checksums).
kernel/native/kernel/core/acpi.h	Every table is length- and checksum-checked before use.

kernel/native/kernel/core/agent_cmd.h	The file is a labelled qualification input, never a user interface, and it can never carry a secret: unlock, rotate, update and shutdown are refused by the parser (the Store secret comes from the card UNLOCK.REQ request, core/unlock_req.h; shutdown is implicit at the end).
kernel/native/kernel/core/allen.c	1 = all checks held.
kernel/native/kernel/core/allen.c	allen.c -- the ALLEN exam stage of the pi5-allen image (PI5-8 cut K): ALLEN REFLEX (crates/allen-reflex, linked as the Rust staticlib crates/allen-reflex-kernel, Makefile CK_ALLEN_LIB) decides every case of the card exam file (core/allen.h) and every decision goes through the ALLEN gate (core/allen_gate.h), which only judges.
kernel/native/kernel/core/allen.c	Gate state: every case is judged against a fresh READY lifecycle (rehearsal: there is no Store in this image and nothing is applied); the stage checks that the judgement left that state byte-for-byte unchanged and that no approve/deny/cancel ever came out PROPOSED.
kernel/native/kernel/core/allen.c	Pass 1 prints one decision line per case, exactly the host harness's line ALLEN_D <mode> <id> <decision json> and one gate line ALLEN_G <mode> <id> <verdict> <verb> ns=<decision ns> Pass 2 decides every case again and compares each line with pass 1 (repeated decisions, amendment 11).
kernel/native/kernel/core/allen_gate.c	TEST-ONLY mutant: ALLEN's approve/deny/cancel would be proposed like any other verb.
kernel/native/kernel/core/allen_gate.h	the command line "<verb>[ <text>]" must pass the card command parser (core/agent_cmd.c: the same verb whitelist and text bounds as a card script, so unlock, rotate, update and shutdown can never pass) and decode to the same lifecycle verb (core/lifecycle_fsm.c), else REJECTED; 4.
kernel/native/kernel/core/allen_gate.h	the lifecycle state machine judges it on a COPY of the caller's state (the caller's state is const and is never changed): a refusal there (for example approve, deny or cancel with nothing pending) is REFUSED with the FSM's own reason; 5.
kernel/native/kernel/core/allen_gate.h	This gate is the only way such a decision may reach the resident lifecycle, and it never performs anything:
kernel/native/kernel/core/allen.h	Nothing is written by the stage: the image has no Store stage and the ALLEN gate (core/allen_gate.h) only judges.
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_card.h	Any deviation refuses the whole file: no entry of a refused file is offered to the loader.
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_card.h	File layout: the existing bundle of tools/ck_artifact_tool.c (command "bundle OUT FILE...", the layout the QEMU gates already use), unchanged, all integers little endian: "AIENBND\0" u32 count (1..CK_ACARD_MAX_ENTRIES) count x { u16 name_len (1..32), name (printable ASCII 0x21..0x7e, unique in the file), u32 byte_len (1..CK_ACARD_ENTRY_MAX), byte_len bytes } and nothing after the last entry.
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_card.h	QUALIFICATION ONLY, TEST keys only.
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_card.h	The loader then runs every check it always runs (format, TEST-key signature, digest, admission, W^X, EL0 containment, receipt): this file proves nothing about who wrote a byte, its bytes are untrusted.
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_card_stage.c	artifact_card_stage.c -- offers the card artifact bundle (core/artifact_card.h) to the core loader through the boot-disk hook (ck_stage_disk_artifacts, ck.h), in the images built with CK_ARTIFACT_CARD only (QUALIFICATION ONLY, TEST keys).
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_loader.c	A test-only QEMU fw_cfg side channel exists only in images built with CK_TEST_FWCFG_ARTIFACTS=1 and is compiled out of every default image.
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_loader.c	Differences from the Rust kernel, all recorded in GATES.md: - one address space at a time on ASID 0 with a full TLB flush on every TTBR0 switch (Rust: TASK_ASID 2); - EL0 FP/SIMD traps (CPACR FPEN = 01) instead of zeroed FP state; - content+table frames and shadow frames are each one contiguous batch; - the loader scheduler is a single occupancy flag (one task at a time, LOADED_TASK_SLOTS = 4 for the availability snapshot, as in Rust).
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_loader.c	OSCUNIT container (OSC_UNIT_ARTIFACT v1): admission verdict only, not a Binary Artifact.
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_loader.c	The ordinary build has no anchors and refuses every candidate.
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_loader.c	The Store never reads a too-large entry, so an OSCUNIT among them cannot be recognised: say so on the osc_unit: channel (RESOURCE_UNAVAILABLE) and keep the old refusal.
kernel/native/kernel/core/artifact_loader.c	Trust: the qualification build (CK_SEED0B_TEST_ANCHOR=1) trusts the RFC 8032 TEST 1 public key ONLY (SEED-0B, TEST ONLY).
kernel/native/kernel/core/console.c	The kernel never writes a UART control register (baud, FIFO, enables stay as the firmware left them).
kernel/native/kernel/core/context.h	cx_open replays every record and refuses the whole journal when one digest or the chain fails; the caller re-reads each record with cx_recall_id and passes its `verified` flag.
kernel/native/kernel/core/context.h	Excluded and counted, never refused: legacy (unbound) records, unpermitted records, unsafe text, records about another Cortex subject or of another class/kind, eligible records past the cap.
kernel/native/kernel/core/context_proposal.h	`approve` rebuilds the bytes from the pending state and commits only when ck_prop_check passes: absent (nothing bound), changed (other bytes or kind) and stale (the Store generation moved since the proposal: something else committed in between) are refused and nothing is written.
kernel/native/kernel/core/entropy.h	Rules: - FEAT_RNG is checked first (ID_AA64ISAR0_EL1.RNDR >= 1); absent means every request is refused.
kernel/native/kernel/core/font8x8.h	Rows only were copied, unchanged; the control characters and DEL are dropped.
kernel/native/kernel/core/handoff_check.c	Checked here: NULL record, magic (CHANDOF3 only; CHANDOF1 and CHANDOF2 are "unsupported version <n>", anything else "bad magic"), reserved0, reserved1, the CHANDOF2 model fields, the CHANDOF3 framebuffer fields, descriptor stride and version, memory map size.
kernel/native/kernel/core/handoff_check.c	handoff_check.c -- refuse a malformed loader -> kernel handoff record before the kernel trusts any field of it (docs/BOOT_HANDOFF_CONTRACT.md section 6).
kernel/native/kernel/core/infer.c	QEMU only: a PASS says nothing about hardware.
kernel/native/kernel/core/infer.c	The traditional byte-at-a-time data register would need ~8e8 trapped MMIO reads under TCG, so this uses the fw_cfg DMA interface: QEMU docs/specs/fw_cfg.rst, "Guest-side DMA Interface" (qemu 8.2.2, /usr/share/doc/qemu-system-data/specs/fw_cfg.rst.gz): feature bit 1 of key 0x0001 and the DMA address register (QEMU CFG signature) at Base + 16 (8 bytes, big endian); a FWCfgDmaAccess{control,length,address} in RAM, all big endian, control bit 1 = read, bit 3 = select (index in the upper 16 bits), bit 0 = error; control reads back 0 when done.
kernel/native/kernel/core/ipc.c	Validate every chain first: a cycle fails before anything is removed.
kernel/native/kernel/core/ipc.h	Port of crates/aienos-kernel/src/caps.rs (CapTable: generation-checked handles, attenuating derive, cross-table derive, tombstones, cascading revoke) and crates/aienos-kernel/src/ipc.rs (bounded FIFO channel of fixed 56-byte messages, two principals, a four-word object reachable only through capabilities).
kernel/native/kernel/core/klog_slot.h	The kernel writes only after it has read the slot's first sector through its own SD driver and found the header with this map's nonce, sector and length (ck_klog_hdr_match): a stale map, a moved file, another card or a wrong sector number all refuse, and nothing is written.
kernel/native/kernel/core/kmain.c	M3 isolation checks (core/m3.c), before any stage registers an IRQ.
kernel/native/kernel/core/kmain.c	One-off check of the EL2 fatal path (never in a normal build).
kernel/native/kernel/core/kmain.c	TEST-ONLY pipe self-test (core/pipe_test.c, scripts/qemu_ck_pipe_test.sh); never in a production image.
kernel/native/kernel/core/kmain.c	They end the candidate boot the two ways a real kernel can fail after "kernel: alive": a CPU exception (BRK at EL1 -> ck_fault_report -> reset) or a hang with interrupts masked (no reset; only an outside reset ends it, docs/BOOT_HANDOFF_CONTRACT.md 7 step 3).
kernel/native/kernel/core/lifecycle.c	Between generation steps the console is polled (no interrupts): a complete line "cancel" abandons the request; any other complete line, and an overlong or damaged one, is refused and never run (only cancel is accepted while generating).
kernel/native/kernel/core/lifecycle.c	Continuity (P05 cut 1): every Store open runs svc/continuity_service.c: identity + ALLEN subject provisioned once, restored and resumed on every boot; a refusal keeps the lifecycle LOCKED.
kernel/native/kernel/core/lifecycle.c	Replay check: 1 when a committed note or reply already carries key, 0 when none does, <0 when a committed object cannot be read (fail closed).
kernel/native/kernel/core/lifecycle.c	the card script is never read while generating: no cancel, no refused lines
kernel/native/kernel/core/lifecycle.c	The parser lets no secret-carrying or control verb through (unlock, rotate, update, shutdown), and the stop order follows the last line (or an absent or refused script) in any case.
kernel/native/kernel/core/lifecycle_fsm.c	unlock carries the optional recovery secret (the binding checks its shape and never echoes it); every other verb takes no argument.
kernel/native/kernel/core/lifecycle_fsm.h	Rotation outcome after CK_LC_R_ROTATE: the state never changes; a refusal by the Store (wrong current secret, no keyslot) counts as refused.
kernel/native/kernel/core/lifecycle_fsm.h	States (native v1 plan P03: initialize, locked/ready, serve, quiesce, stop): INIT before the lifecycle took over from the boot stages LOCKED production Store not open (no production key source yet, or it refused): status/unlock/help/shutdown only; every task refused READY Store open: a task may be submitted SERVE one bounded task pending: approve commits it, deny or cancel drops it QUIESCE stop order running: flush, revoke, halt STOP nothing more is accepted Verbs are the v1 operator surface and nothing else: status, unlock, task <text>, approve, deny, cancel, evidence, help, shutdown, and (P06) prompt <text>, recall, remember <text>, facts, and (N3, aienos#329) prefer <text>.
kernel/native/kernel/core/line_rx.h	Every other complete line (a verb other than cancel, an overlong or damaged line) is refused: refuse(verb, reason) is called and the line is never run.
kernel/native/kernel/core/line_rx.h	Rules (feed one byte, or CK_LINE_RX_ERR for a byte the UART dropped): - printable ASCII 0x20..0x7e is collected, up to CK_LC_LINE_MAX bytes; - Backspace (0x08) or DEL (0x7f) removes the last byte; - CR or LF ends the line (a CR LF pair gives one line and one empty line); - any other control byte is dropped and counted; - a line past CK_LC_LINE_MAX is refused whole at its Enter (OVERLONG), the rest of it is dropped until then; - a line during which the UART reported a lost or damaged byte is refused whole at its Enter (DAMAGED): a command with a missing byte never runs.
kernel/native/kernel/core/m3.c	Prints, in the Rust order: threads: two cooperative EL1 threads on the core/sched.c run queue el0: an EL0 task in its own address space: capability-checked write, forged handle refused, kernel-address read faults and is contained preempt: two spinning EL1 workers switched only by the timer IRQ placement: workers placed on cores from the MADT efficiency classes (pure computation: neither kernel starts a secondary core) ipc: two EL0 principals, typed message, attenuated delegation, forged handle refused, revoked handle refused Single core, EL1h; runs with IRQs masked except inside the preempt check and while EL0 runs.
kernel/native/kernel/core/osc_admit.c	aienos#383 receipt self-test: every launch of a01_valid_min.unit add(a, b), each with one receipt from the Store (tests/fixtures/osc_receipt, minted by omega checker2 with its TEST key; a01.owner.rcpt, an OWNER receipt for the same unit signed with the owner key, tests/fixtures/osc_receipt_owner, aienos#387) or none.

kernel/native/kernel/dev/devices.c	a failed bind never keeps DMA
kernel/native/kernel/dev/devices.c	A refused abort leaves the stream confined (or bus mastering on): that is LIVE, never released (#489).
kernel/native/kernel/dev/devices.h	Ownership is returned only when bus mastering is off and the SMMU stream is back to abort; a refused abort keeps the stream confined and the function owned, and a later call retries.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/disk_layout.h	Everything below is INSIDE the AIENOS GPT partition (dev/disk_part.h): unit numbers are relative to the partition's first LBA and every access goes through the bounds-checked translation layer ck_part_xlate.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/disk_part.c	disk_part.c -- read-only GPT parser and the single bounds-checked LBA translation layer of the AIENOS C kernel (see disk_part.h).
kernel/native/kernel/dev/disk_part.c	Stream the entry array at lba: CRC32 over num*esize bytes; when scan is set, also check every used entry and find the one entry of `type`.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/disk_part.h	Every kernel disk access after that (rw probe, torn_slot anchor, Store) goes through ONE bounds-checked translation layer, ck_part_xlate, behind a disk_dev "partition view" (ck_part.dev): partition-relative LBA in, absolute LBA out, refused unless the whole transfer lies inside the partition.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/disk_part.h	On success fills *p (a valid partition view over d) and *info; on failure *p is invalid (every I/O through p->dev refused) and the CK_GPT_* code is returned.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/disk_part.h	TEST-ONLY mutation (scripts/qemu_ck_disk_layout_test.sh): the translation layer adds no partition offset and checks only the parent disk's bounds.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/disk_part.h	Validation is strict and fails closed (no fallback to the whole disk): protective MBR (0x55AA, one 0xEE record), primary header (signature, revision 1.0, size, CRC32, MyLBA 1, AlternateLBA = last LBA, usable range), primary entry array CRC32, backup header (CRC32, MyLBA = last, AlternateLBA 1, same disk GUID / entry geometry / entry CRC) and the backup entry array CRC32; every used entry inside the usable range, no two used entries overlapping, exactly one AIENOS entry.

kernel/native/kernel/dev/dma_window.c	unused only in the NO_GOT mutant
kernel/native/kernel/dev/dma_window.h	Any violation latches the window; a latched window refuses everything.

kernel/native/kernel/dev/mmio_window.c	Exclusive: a page some other driver already mapped is refused, so the unmap on revoke can never pull a mapping out from under it.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/mmio_window.h	A bare ck_cap_revoke removes the capability and the accessor refuses, but the window is never told, so its pages stay mapped (tested in stage_test.c).
kernel/native/kernel/dev/mmio_window.h	After revoke every read through the old handle or any derived child is refused with CK_CAP_INVALID.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/mmio_window.h	exclusive map refused: a page is already mapped, overlaps RAM, or cannot be mapped
kernel/native/kernel/dev/mmio_window.h	Exclusivity holds in both directions: while a window is live, the kernel's ck_mm_mmio_try_map refuses (NULL) and ck_mmio_map panics for any range that overlaps its pages, so a driver cannot share them later either.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/mmio_window.h	It maps them with ck_mmio_map_exclusive, which refuses (fail closed) when any page is already mapped, so a mapping some other driver holds (nvme_bind maps the same NVMe BAR0) can never be unmapped by a window.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/mmio_window.h	Limits (named): BAR size is not discoverable here, so the caller supplies it from firmware or a fixture; the mapping is Device RW even though the accessor is read-only (no read-only Device mapping is made); revoke must go through ck_mmio_revoke (see RULE above); the exclusive-range table holds 8 ranges and a ninth window is refused with CK_MMIO_E_MAP; no interrupts, no DMA, no polling helper, no config-space or COMMAND changes.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/mmio_window.h	- rights are READ, DERIVE, REVOKE only; WRITE, MAP and GRANT are refused at creation, and derive cannot add a right the parent lacks; - there is NO write accessor for this object; - ck_mmio_read validates the handle (live, current generation, READ right, this window's resource) and then offset/width, all BEFORE the device is touched.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/net_bind.c	After the negative test: reset + re-init (vnet_init writes status 0 and rebuilds every descriptor inside the window) and a second full round trip, still confined, with no new SMMU fault.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/net_bind.c	Events the refused probe raised after the negative test drained the queue belong to the probe: drop them (reported) so new_faults below counts only the second round trip.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/net_bind.c	Pass: the SMMU event queue holds an F_TRANSLATION record for this stream at that page, the page keeps its pattern, and the positive round trip before it raised no fault at all.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/net_bind.h	Attach (DMA gate, BAR map, bus master, vnet_init_flags with ACCESS_PLATFORM required), then ARP for the gateway and one UDP datagram to CK_NET_ECHO_PORT and back, then the SMMU fence test and its recovery round trip (CK_NET_E_SMMU if either fails), every wait bounded by ck_time_us.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/net_bind.h	Measured on QEMU 8.2.2: no NEEDS_RESET; QEMU completes the RX through its internal bounce buffer (rx_completed=1) while the refused write never reaches the page.

kernel/native/kernel/dev/nvme_bind.c	Any of these not refused: stop before any write.

kernel/native/kernel/dev/nvme_bind.c	Pass: the SMMU records a translation fault for this stream at that page, the page keeps its pattern (the write never landed), and after restoring the bounce address a normal read works again.
kernel/native/kernel/dev/nvme_bind.c	Refused before any write to the device: as if the bind was never attempted.

kernel/native/kernel/dev/nvme_bind.h	gate DMA, map BAR0, init the controller, fill n->disk, print identify/geometry/atomicity lines, run the read-only checks (bounds refusal, read LBA 0), parse the GPT read-only and select the AIENOS partition (n->part; none or an invalid GPT: print "disk: no AIENOS partition, refusing writes" and return the CK_GPT_* code with nothing written), check the translation layer refuses past the partition end, then run the write+flush+read-back probe on the last 4 KiB unit OF THE PARTITION.

kernel/native/kernel/GATES.md	`open` binds the model once and allocates the KV cache for `kv_capacity` positions; `prompt` applies the chat template, refuses a prompt over `max_prompt_bytes` (-16) or one that would not fit the cache (-8) or the heap reserve (-15), and resets the decode state; `step` does exactly one unit of work per call (one prompt token into the cache during prefill, then one greedy token plus its forward) and returns PREFILL/TOKEN/LAST/DONE, so the kernel can cancel between any two steps; `cancel` forgets the cache and returns the count produced; `close` drops everything, after which the kernel forgets the heap region.

kernel/native/kernel/gpu/gmmu.h	All-or-nothing: on refusal no entry is left changed.
kernel/native/kernel/gpu/gmmu.h	Refuses: zero size, wrap, misalignment, VA >= 2^57, PA outside the window, any page already mapped (any size), a 2 MiB page over an existing page table, small/big pages under an existing 2 MiB PTE, pool exhaustion.
kernel/native/kernel/gpu/gmmu.h	Unmap a range mapped with `page`; refuses (no change) unless every page in it is mapped with that page size.
kernel/native/kernel/gpu/gpu_mem.h	pa_base: start of the data pages (inside as->win, 4 KiB aligned, not overlapping the page-table pool); va_base: GPU VA of page 0 (2 MiB aligned, so a page's VA never equals its CPU address by accident: the tests also check that directly).

kernel/native/kernel/gpu/gsp_sim.c	Anything else: SIM-CHOICE, SEC2 consumes it and never releases the target mask (a refused chain of trust).

kernel/native/kernel/proofs/checker/Inspect.lean	check.sh hashes type and value and compares every line with the committed proofs/TicketLock.pins.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/checker/Syntax.lean	Parses FILE with Lean's own parser and prints every syntax node kind it contains, one `KIND <name>` line each, sorted and unique, so check.sh can refuse any kind outside the committed allowlist (proofs/TicketLock.syntax).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/checker/Syntax.lean	Parsing only: nothing in FILE is elaborated or run here, and check.sh does not let lean elaborate FILE unless every kind is allowed.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/checker/Syntax.lean	The header's imports are not processed: the file is parsed against Init alone (the prelude every file gets), and an `import` shows up as a Lean.Parser.Module.import kind, which the allowlist refuses.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/check.sh	A control with an "expect-unrestricted:" line is run again with the syntax rule off (check.sh --control-unrestricted, whose last line is CK_TICKETLOCK_PROOF_CONTROL, never the PASS line) and must FAIL with those rules too, so the later rules catch it on their own.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/check.sh	Last line CK_TICKETLOCK_PROOF: PASS (exit 0), FAIL (exit 1), or NOT_RUN (exit 3: no lean or leanchecker, or a lean other than the pinned one; never a pass).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/check.sh	proofs/check.sh --selftest FILE.lean: every control in proofs/controls/ must FAIL with every rule its "expect:" line names: a .lean control is appended to a copy of FILE, a .sed control is a sed script run on a copy (it must change it).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/check.sh	Run lean from proofs/ so elan picks the toolchain proofs/lean-toolchain names; refuse any other lean.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/check.sh	When this rule fails lean never runs FILE, so code in FILE never runs; lean lean (the toolchain proofs/lean-toolchain names) compiles FILE as module TicketLock to an .olean with exit 0 and no warning or error; kernel leanchecker (same toolchain, a separate process) replays every constant of that .olean through Lean's kernel, so a declaration added without kernel checking fails here; constants checker/Inspect.lean (a separate process that imports the .olean) lists every constant the module declares, generated ones included; none is an axiom or unsafe, and each uses only propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound; pins those constants equal proofs/TicketLock.pins exactly: the same names (none missing, none extra), kinds, elaborated types (sha256 of Lean's repr), definition bodies (sha256) and theorem statements (pretty-printed).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/controls/admit.lean	Negative control for proofs/check.sh (appended to a copy of TicketLock.lean; never part of the proof): a theorem closed by admit (Lean warns "declaration uses 'sorry'" and still exits 0).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/controls/attribute.lean	Negative control for proofs/check.sh (appended to a copy of TicketLock.lean; never part of the proof): an attribute command, outside the allowlist (attributes can attach compiled code or rewrite rules); no banned word, so only the syntax rule sees it.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/controls/axiom_false.lean	Negative control for proofs/check.sh (appended to a copy of TicketLock.lean; never part of the proof): an axiom of False and a theorem proved from it.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/controls/extra_theorem.lean	Negative control for proofs/check.sh (appended to a copy of TicketLock.lean; never part of the proof): a true, harmless theorem with allowed syntax and a valid proof.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/controls/macro_hidden.lean	Negative control for proofs/check.sh (appended to a copy of TicketLock.lean; never part of the proof): a theorem made by a macro, so no line of the file starts with "theorem" for it and it gets no `#print axioms` line; only Lean's own listing of the file's constants sees it.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/controls/native_decide.lean	Negative control for proofs/check.sh (appended to a copy of TicketLock.lean; never part of the proof): a theorem closed by native_decide (trusts the compiler: axiom Lean.ofReduceBool; no warning).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/controls/run_tac.lean	Negative control for proofs/check.sh (appended to a copy of TicketLock.lean; never part of the proof): a proof by a tactic that runs arbitrary code (run_tac), which the allowlist does not list.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/controls/set_option.lean	Negative control for proofs/check.sh (appended to a copy of TicketLock.lean; never part of the proof): a set_option command (options can switch off checks; the allowlist has no set_option kind).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/TicketLock.lean	Every outstanding caller holds a ticket t with serving ≤ t < ticket (it took the old value of ticket after serving had passed nobody it had not served).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/TicketLock.lean	proofs/check.sh (make ticketlock-proof) refuses this file unless every constant in it uses only propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/TicketLock.lean	(Reach/reach_le/enters_iff_head are the smaller lemmas these use.) * the interrupt ordering of core/spinlock.h in a one-CPU model checked by `decide` over the concrete lock programs (lockUnlock_safe, nested_safe, lockUnlock_restores), and each TEST-ONLY mutant of the header unsafe (ticketBeforeMask_unsafe, restoreBeforeRelease_unsafe, noMask_unsafe); * the same on two CPUs under every interleaving, with an interrupt transition whose handler takes the same lock, for both forms of the header: the explicit pair and ck_spin_lock / ck_spin_unlock, which keep the saved state in the lock (l->irq) and so must write it after entry and read it before the release (slot_safe, pair_safe, slot_pair_safe; the five mutants *_unsafe fail, see the last section).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/TicketLock.lean	`runs2` checks, for the four initial mask states and every schedule: * nothing gets stuck: no interrupt lands on a CPU with a ticket outstanding, and some CPU can always step until both programs are done; * mutual exclusion: the two CPUs are never inside at once; * each CPU ends with the mask state it started with (a pre-masked caller stays masked).
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/TicketLock.lean	So a program is safe when every state it passes through has: outstanding > 0 → masked.
kernel/native/kernel/proofs/TicketLock.lean	What ties them to the C: tests/test_spinlock.c runs the same mutants on the host (make spinlock-mutant), and tools/ck_spinlock_asm_check.sh checks the compiled order of both forms and of every lock site in core/pipe.c (make spinlock-asm-check).
kernel/native/kernel/README.md	A `CK_SERVICE` image instead keeps the NVMe bound and comes up LOCKED; the operator types `unlock <64-hex recovery secret>` on the serial console and `ck_store_unlock_boot` (`svc/store_boot.c`) runs the ADR 0017 section 2.4 Slot 1 path: on a blank disk it draws K_vol, salt and nonce from the entropy service (refused when RNDR is), wraps K_vol into a Recovery_RawSecret descriptor (HKDF-SHA256 KEK) and commits the KeySlotManifest (kind 21) with the first boot record; on a non-blank disk it reads the manifest before any key exists, unwraps K_vol and opens the Store with PRODUCTION-class keys.
kernel/native/kernel/README.md	Any `report_kind: panic` or `report_kind: fault`, a missing `bus master revoked` line, or a keyboard that is ready but ignores typing is a FAIL to report, not to retry.
kernel/native/kernel/README.md	Awake, the hold loop polls devices at most once every 0.5 ms by the TSC, not on every pass: the earlier device-read storm starved QEMU after keyboard replug.
kernel/native/kernel/README.md	A wrong secret fails the AES-GCM-SIV tag before the disk is opened; the secret is echoed as `*` and never printed.

kernel/native/kernel/README.md	The Store, torn-slot device and rw probe get only that partition's `disk_dev` view, whose every read, write and flush goes through `ck_part_xlate` (bounds-checked partition LBA -> disk LBA).
kernel/native/kernel/README.md	TLB shootdown (#485): `invlpg` flushes only the core that runs it, and the started cores keep running, so every page table change that takes rights away or unmaps (`tlb_flush_page`, the trampoline's included) also sends an IPI (vector 0xf0) to every secondary core that has checked in and waits until each has flushed the page and acknowledged; a core that does not answer within a second is a panic.

kernel/native/kernel/README.md	- **Versioned op table.** `ck_pipe_dispatch` takes a platform call frame (magic `OSHF`, `CK_PIPE_ABI_VERSION` 1, op id, reserved, arguments) and refuses malformed frames and unknown ops or versions (INVALID_ARG, NOT_SUPPORTED) before any effect.
kernel/native/kernel/README.md	**What the Spark will print** (INFERRED from those tables, high confidence; hardware NOT_RUN): the walker finds all six controllers, and every one is then **denied** with `dma_gate: xhci USBn denied (SmmuNotReady), controller left halted`.
kernel/native/kernel/README.md	`x86/efi_x86.c` is the UEFI stub (GOP via `../boot/efi_gop.c`, RSDP, memory map, ExitBootServices) and hands over the same CHANDOF3 record, checked by `core/handoff_check.c`; `x86/kmain_x86.c` loads its own GDT, TSS and IDT (`x86/trap.S`), builds its own identity page tables (2 MiB pages: RAM write-back, everything else uncached, the framebuffer write-combining, a 4 KiB guard page under the stack, physical page 0 not mapped so a NULL dereference faults), proves the guard page and NULL reads and writes fault and the faults are contained, measures the TSC against the HPET, runs the local APIC timer (xAPIC) for 20 interrupts at 100 Hz, starts every other core the MADT lists (INIT-SIPI-SIPI into the `x86/smp_x86.S` trampoline below 1 MiB; each core loads the kernel's tables, turns interrupts on, checks in and draws or halts), then walks PCI (`x86/pci_x86.c`, MCFG ECAM window; device registers above the identity map are mapped on demand) and reads every disk without writing.

kernel/native/kernel/svc/allen_code.h	1 when it is a CODE line for rx's job and was kept (a piece already held is kept again, no harm), 0 when the line is not a CODE line at all, -1 when it is a CODE line that does not fit (another job, a malformed head, a piece past the limits or of the wrong length, a head that disagrees with the first piece's): counted in rx->refused.

kernel/native/kernel/svc/allen_memory.h	A copy is valid only when every field checks out: magic, version, exact length, fingerprint, fact count, NUL-terminated folded text (a-z, 0-9, single spaces), a name of letters, and v4+ sky fields in range (mode 0..2, region 0..11, have_loc 0..1, pad 0, latitude within +-90 and longitude within +-180 degrees).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/allen_memory.h	A note that fails any check loads as gone and is counted in *damaged.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/allen_memory.h	One copy is one unit: 0 "AIENALM1" 8 bytes 8 version (2..4) u32 little endian 12 payload length u32 16 generation u64, +1 on every save 24 zero 8 bytes 32 SHA-256 of bytes 0..31 and the payload 64 payload: starts u32, lines u32, name[24], fact count u32, note count u32, then per fact key[48] val[64] are(u8); v3+: self[24]; v4+: sky_mode(u8) sky_region(u8) sky_have_loc(u8) pad(u8) lat_udeg(s32) lon_udeg(s32); A save writes generation g+1 over the OLDER copy, flushes the disk's write cache, then reads it back and checks it; the newer copy is never touched until the older one holds the newer state.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/allen_reply.c	A question whose every key word is in one note ("what's the gate code").
kernel/native/kernel/svc/allen_scene.h	Integer only, freestanding; host tested and fuzzed in ui/tests/canvas_test.c.

kernel/native/kernel/svc/compat/README	Every name is redirected to a ck_compat_* function in ../ck_compat.c, built on ck.h services: malloc/calloc/free -> ck_alloc / ck_free clock_gettime -> ck_time_us fopen("/dev/urandom") + fread -> ck_entropy_fill (arch/rndr.c over core/entropy.c): the CPU RNDR instruction (FEAT_RNG checked, bounded retry, stuck test) or a refusal: fread returns 0 and the capability office token fails closed.

kernel/native/kernel/svc/continuity_boot.h	Mode selection (contract K-7, DECIDED): the host writes one ASCII line into the last 4096-byte unit of the AIENOS partition (the rw-probe scratch unit, which the devices stage restores byte for byte, dev/nvme_bind.c ck_disk_rw_probe; the same carrier as svc/store_crash.h): AIENCONT v1 mode=<N>[ cp=<checkpoint>][ response=<64 hex>]\n mode uses the numbers of the Rust control block (store_qual.rs:27-48): 1 ordinary Store boot (formats a blank disk, commits a boot record): used by the harness to make "a formatted Store with data, no identity" 5 provision (formats a blank disk first, as the Rust mode 5) 6 resume 7 resume + remember 8 resume + commit with a crash point 9 Recovery Core inspect 10 inspect + repair-degraded-peer with `response` 11 inspect + provision-identity with `response` cp (mode 8 only): the C checkpoint where the kernel halts after printing "CHECKPOINT: <name>" (the host then kills QEMU): before_first_write, after_payload_objects, after_catalog, after_commit_record, after_first_flush, after_inactive_superblock, after_final_flush, before_anchor, after_anchor.

kernel/native/kernel/svc/continuity_codec.c	The scan is capped at CC_MAX_BRANCHES: a table that passes the fork-sum check below has every forks <= 255, so the cap changes no accept/refuse outcome, only bounds the work (Rust scans up to forks, which an input controls; see the PR's oracle notes).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/continuity_codec.c	The sum is checked: an overflowing sum can never equal children (<= 255).

kernel/native/kernel/svc/continuity_recovery.h	Fails toward preservation: - repair only a Malformed peer and only with a resolved identity (MR-7); - provision only on a Valid mount that reads Unprovisioned (MR-5): a degraded mount is never treated as unprovisioned; - everything else (sealed refusals, conflicts, corrupt continuity, unformatted stores, a CRC-valid newer root whose graph is broken) admits nothing.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/continuity_recovery.h	RC_SEALED_REFUSAL is C only (contract K-3/K-4): the sealed mount (keyed proof, anti-rollback anchor, envelope or transaction record) refused before continuity could run.

kernel/native/kernel/svc/continuity_subject.c	---- decode: one bounds check, every rule, no trailing bytes ----
kernel/native/kernel/svc/continuity_subject.c	Pass 1: every kind-24 object of this Store into w->t (id, seq, previous), each one decoded and checked against the resolved root.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/continuity_subject_provision.c	It accepts exactly one call carrying exactly one AgentRoot; anything else is refused before the inner sink is touched, so a provisioning write without its subject cannot reach the Store.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/continuity_subject_provision.h	Refusals, in order: null or invalid genesis input (CR_E_ARG, nothing read); kind-24 objects already in the Store (CR_CORRUPT "subject objects without an agent root" when there is no root; a Store with a root is CR_ALREADY_PROVISIONED from cr_provision); then every cr_provision refusal unchanged.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/cortex_store.c	The source is vendored byte for byte from the omega.lock commit (third_party/omega/rx_cortex.c, checked by scripts/omega_lock_check.sh); this wrapper only sets the build switches so the vendored file stays identical to Omega's.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/pi5_agent.c	TEST-ONLY mutant: recovery never looks at the Store
kernel/native/kernel/svc/pi5_agent.h	1: the reply was recovered/committed and passed every check
kernel/native/kernel/svc/pi5_agent.h	Returns 0 when the stage did what its mode says and every check passed (NOSTORE counts: it is a refusal, not a failure of this stage); <0 otherwise (printed).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/pi5_agent.h	The resident model is ingested and hashed (core/infer.c ck_infer_run, the same path as the QEMU service image), one bounded prompt runs (ck_infer_prompt), the session is closed, and only a reply that passes every golden check is committed as one sealed Store object of the lifecycle's reply kind 'R1' (core/reply_obj.h, shared with core/lifecycle.c) plus a model/ids trailer.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/pi5_fact.c	Every fact, oldest first (label "readback" right after a remember in the same boot, "recalled" on a later boot), each read back through cx_recall_id (digest re-checked); then the chain.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/pi5_fact.c	TEST-ONLY mutant: recall never looks at the journal.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/pi5_fact.h	After the Store stage opened the sealed Store through the Slot 1 recovery path, this stage does what the service lifecycle's `remember` and `facts` verbs do (core/lifecycle.c, QEMU rows 179-181), with the same Cortex records on the same journal ('CJ' chunks, one record = one sealed transaction): - a fact request from the card (core/fact_req.h): open or create the journal, append one CX_CLAIM about the owner subject, read it back, print it with the Store generation that committed it; - no request: replay the journal, re-check every digest and the chain, print each fact with the Store generation it was committed at.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/setup_store.h	One copy is one unit (4096 bytes): 0 "AIENSET1" 8 bytes 8 version (1) u32 little endian 12 text length u32 (at most CK_SETUP_TEXT) 16 generation u64, +1 on every save 24 zero 8 bytes 32 SHA-256 of bytes 0..31 and the text 64 the text: printable ASCII and newlines (svc/setup_wizard.h: the saved step, then llm.conf and the consent record, each starting with its own first line); never a key: sw_save and sw_llm_conf hold none 64+len zero to the end of the unit (a copy with any other byte there is not valid: the SHA-256 does not cover it) A save writes generation g+1 over the older copy, flushes, reads it back and checks it.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/setup_wizard.h	courier: 1 when a wire to the host is there to test a key (without one, Allen says he cannot keep a key safely yet).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/setup_wizard.h	hello -> the one question (games / an AI model / something else) -> getting to know this computer (what setup_inv.h found, line by line) -> the one consent (Set it up / Show me each one / Not now) -> the intent path (games: screen, graphics, processor, what cannot be measured yet; AI: the graphics memory and which models fit, from their measured sizes on disk) -> which AI (found here, found nearby, or connect my own: Claude, ChatGPT, another service; the key is tested with one request) -> "Allen is ready".
kernel/native/kernel/svc/setup_wizard.h	The clock: steps that move on by themselves (hello, the scan, the 10 s auto-pick of a single model, a test that never answers).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/setup_wizard.h	The key the person types lives only in this struct; sw_forget_key wipes it once the test is done, and it is never written into any record.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/slot_service.h	Every write is one control record commit through the torn-slot protocol (ck_slot_control_commit, the same routine as the loader) over the slots partition view (dev/devices.c ck_dev_slots_disk); the decision is the pure ck_slot_kernel_action (native/boot/slot_select.c).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.c	Before any disk access: a hardware staging build never opens, and never formats a blank disk, under TEST-identity keys.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.c	No TEST keys, no TEST uuid are compiled, and the policy is the hardware one: TEST-identity keys are refused before any disk access.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.c	Production service image without owner provisioning: the production key source only (it refuses today: no in-kernel K_vol unwrap, P04).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	1 if keys of this identity class may open/format a Store in this build: PRODUCTION always; TEST only when hardware_staging is 0.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	A refusal names which proof failed and never reformats a non-blank disk: structural unkeyed Store::open (st_open) refused the region keyed M5 identity/records/envelopes refused under the given keys rollback the anti-rollback anchor refused the Store's generation io the disk failed Only an all-zero anchor + Store head counts as blank and is formatted.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	A Store with history but no manifest, or a manifest failing any check, is refused (CK_SB_E_SECMAN) before the boot record is written and the keys are wiped.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	ck_store_pi5_unlock_run: NULL secret refuses before any disk read; else the Slot 1 path with the hardware policy (TEST keys and TEST uuid do not exist in this image).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	ck_store_unlock_boot takes the operator's 32-byte raw recovery secret and never any key material from the image: on a blank disk it draws K_vol, salt and nonce from the kernel entropy service (RNDR; refused when RNDR is), wraps K_vol into a Recovery_RawSecret descriptor (slot_id 1, HKDF KEK) and commits the KeySlotManifest (kind 21, plaintext, bound by the sealed transaction record) in the same first transaction as the boot record.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	Every boot transaction also commits one SecurityManifest (kind 22, a sealed envelope whose root_mac is under k_root_auth): generation = the generation the transaction creates, security_sequence = previous + 1 (1 at genesis), previous_security_manifest_id = the ObjectId of the manifest it supersedes (zero at genesis), key_epoch = the owner hierarchy generation of the open keys, keyslot_manifest_id = the KeySlotManifest ObjectId (zero for a TEST-key Store, which has none), epoch = generation (the sealed Store's anti-rollback anchor counts Store generations).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	Same rule as the fact stage: the Store is passed on only when it committed, so a refused Store (wrong secret) never reaches the model.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	store_boot_run refuses TEST-identity keys in that build before any disk access, so it never formats a blank disk with them.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	The open Store of the last store_boot_run (valid only after it returned 0; host image tool and tests write further objects through it).
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	The Store is passed on only when it committed; a refused Store (wrong secret) recalls nothing.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_boot.h	They protect nothing and every line printed with them says TEST.
kernel/native/kernel/svc/store_crash.h	Compiled only into the TEST image "make full CK_TEST_STORE_CRASH=1" ($(OUT)/full-test-store-crash/).

kernel/native/kernel/tools/ck_manifest_sig.c	The verifier rebuilds those bytes from the manifest it is given and checks a plain Ed25519 signature over them, so any change to the manifest changes the statement and the signature fails.

kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	A started core loads the kernel's GDT (no task register) and IDT, sets the same PAT, enables its local APIC, records its APIC ID, turns interrupts on (only IPIs reach it: its LVT entries are masked, the I/O APIC lines too), checks in, and runs the night's drawing or halts.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	Every pass of a drawing core ends in a PAUSE, a frame drawn or not.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	invlpg drops a translation from this core's TLB only, so a change that takes rights from a page (or unmaps it) is not done until every core that may hold the old translation has dropped it too: the page is flushed here, VEC_TLB goes to every secondary core that has checked in, and this returns once each has flushed it and acknowledged.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	Lab image only: restart instead of powering off, so the iMac comes back to its network loader (NETLDR.EFI) for the next test without a hand on the power button.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	Never guess Intel/AMD registers when the existing AMD path is unsupported or fails.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	Polling them on every pass hit them about a million times a second, which under QEMU starved the emulator's main loop of its big lock after a keyboard replug (handoff 2026-10-18: the replugged keyboard's keys never arrived).
kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	QEMU swap test only (never in a lab image that reaches the iMac): teach Allen one fact through the real line path so kernel A writes Allen's memory to disk before the swap, and the swapped-in kernel must load it back.

kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	The picture's frame (when this core draws it) and the sleep bookkeeping run every pass.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	---- TLB shootdown self-test (#485) ---- On each secondary core in turn: the core writes tlb_page (so it caches a writable translation), the boot core makes the page read-only and shoots it down, and the core's next write must fault; the core reads the page (a fresh read-only translation), the boot core unmaps it and shoots it down, and the core's next read must fault.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	When the water came (f913a63) a frame under QEMU TCG took longer than FRAME_MS, so the frame core drew frame after frame and never reached its PAUSE: a virtual CPU that never leaves its translated code never passes an RCU quiescent point, and QEMU's device_add waits for one (drain_call_rcu).
kernel/native/kernel/x86/kmain_x86.c	W^X negative controls (make IMACKERN_WX_MUTANT=..., QEMU only, never deployed): no_nx drops every NX bit, text_writable leaves the code writable, ap_no_wp starts the secondary cores without CR0.WP (smp_x86.S), no_shootdown leaves the other cores' TLBs stale (invlpg on the boot core only).
kernel/native/kernel/x86/memory_x86.c	Its manifest signature and image SHA-512 are checked again here against the kernel's configured owner keys, before any boot-section write window is opened.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/memory_x86.c	The network is unauthenticated, so an older owner-signed kernel can be replayed: a kernel whose generation is below the installed kernel's policy_generation (the floor that kernel set, slot_format.h) is refused, as is any install when the installed manifest cannot be read.

kernel/native/kernel/x86/tests/swap_disk_bound_test.c	Overflow-safe: lengths near the top of u64 and size_t are refused, never wrapped.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/x86.h	pci_x86.c: PCI walk over the MCFG ECAM window; every AHCI and NVMe disk read-only (identify, read, GPT check), a look at the Ethernet chip.
kernel/native/kernel/x86/xhci_x86.c	A failed discovery, or a failed report from a keyboard that stays plugged in, is retried up to KBD_ERR_MAX times; after that only a real connect change (unplug, plug back in) starts discovery again, so a faulty keyboard cannot keep every port in reset.
kernel/native/linux/aien-gpu/aien_gpu.c	evidence/own-gpu-driver/rung2/window1_sim_test.sh checks these lists against every access the start-up tool makes on the simulator.
kernel/native/linux/aien-gpu/aien_gpu_check.c	Exit 0 = all checks passed.
kernel/native/linux/aien-gpu/aien_gpu.c	The SEC2 doorbell (QUEUE_HEAD(0)) can be rung once per load: a failed start-up is never retried without a reset.
kernel/native/linux/aien-gpu/aien_gpu.c	What it does, and nothing more: - binds the GB10 (10de:2e12) ONLY when loaded with allow_bind=1 (nvidia.ko must be unloaded first: two drivers on one GPU is the one thing that must never happen); - maps BAR0 and READS two identity registers (NV_PMC_BOOT_0 at 0x0, NV_PMC_BOOT_42 at 0xA00): no write; - loads the GSP firmware file on loan from NVIDIA (nvidia/580.173.02/gsp_ga10x.bin) and checks its size; it does not send it to the GPU yet; - allocates one DMA-coherent arena (arena_mib, default 2) and offers it to user space by mmap of /dev/aien-gpu, the shape of the memory interface Omega gets (one big mapping); - AIEN_GPU_INFO ioctl returns what it read; - window 1: a second DMA-coherent buffer for the GSP sysmem heap (heap_mib, default 0, never mapped to user space) and AIEN_GPU_REG_RD / AIEN_GPU_REG_WR for exactly the registers of the GSP start-up contract (gpu/contract/gb10_gsp.devc).
kernel/native/linux/aien-gpu/aien_gsp_boot.c	aien_gsp_boot --pkg DIR --app-version N --out DIR [--polls N] [--pause-us N] [--wait-ms N] [--plan-only] sim build only: [--sim-events init|seq|none|junk] [--sim-cfg happy|busy|fmc-error|riscv-dead] [--sim-arena-mib N] Exit 0 = INIT-DONE (or PLAN-ONLY), 1 = the chip stopped somewhere (verdict says where), 2 = refused before the chip.
kernel/native/linux/aien-gpu/aien_gsp_boot.c	every loaded package file stays reachable until exit (one-shot tool; the sanitized sim build checks for leaks)
kernel/native/linux/aien-gpu/aien_gsp_boot.c	Pass or fail, all of it is evidence.
kernel/native/linux/aien-gpu/aien_gsp_boot.c	writes every register access with its time, every status message, the GSP log buffers and a one-line verdict (AIEN_GSP_BOOT: ...) into the output directory.
kernel/native/linux/aien-gpu/README.md	Today it binds the GB10 only when loaded with `allow_bind=1` (never while nvidia.ko owns the GPU), reads two identity registers (no write), loads NVIDIA's GSP firmware file and checks its size, allocates one DMA-coherent arena and offers it by mmap of /dev/aien-gpu.
kernel/native/m5/m5_envelope.c	Private scratchpad: a chunk is decrypted here, its tag checked, and only then copied to the caller (AGENTS.md section 4).
kernel/native/m5/m5_envelope.c	Truncation and extension are refused because total_plaintext_len is in every chunk's AAD and the length must match it exactly.
kernel/native/m5/m5.h	A manifest sealed under one class or owner generation fails the MAC under any other.
kernel/native/m5/m5.h	Bounded header parse: every length field is checked before use and env_len must equal the length the header implies.
kernel/native/m5/m5.h	Every descriptor passes m5_keyslot_decode; payload ranges must lie inside the arena.
kernel/native/m5/m5.h	Refuses a malformed body, a body whose signature field is not all zero (M5_ERR_FORMAT) and a key whose public key does not match owner_key_id (M5_ERR_BINDING).
kernel/native/m5/m5.h	Verifies a signed record against the trusted owner public key and the verifier's expectations (every field except owner_key_id and migration_counter must equal expect's), then refuses a counter not above last_migration_counter (M5_ERR_REPLAY).
kernel/native/m5/m5_keys.c	Value checks shared by decode and unwrap, so a struct built in memory cannot bypass what the serialized form would be refused for.
kernel/native/m5/Makefile	The tests must fail (or crash) for every mutant, or that rule is not tested.
kernel/native/m5/README.md	| All-or-nothing open | `m5_envelope_open`, `m5_recover_object` zero the whole caller buffer on every error | `t_all_or_nothing_late_chunk_failure_zeroes_output`; every refusal test asserts an all-zero buffer |
kernel/native/m5/README.md	All tests in this folder are host-only (hosted libc test program), and nothing measures time or energy.
kernel/native/m5/README.md	Decode and unwrap also refuse a wrap_suite other than 0x01 (Empty may hold 0), kdf_suite above 0x02 and any flag bit (ADR 0017; Rust does not check these).
kernel/native/m5/README.md	- **Owner-signature binding: host-tested with TEST keys only; the real owner signature is BLOCKED_OPERATOR on the Gate 3 offline key ceremony.** The signature primitive is `native/sig` (in-house Ed25519, #188).
kernel/native/m5/README.md	| Production/test identity separation | identity class in the hierarchy key (so in every object key and K_root_auth MAC), plus an explicit class byte in commit record, anchor and migration; open/verify take a mode | `t_identity_production_refuses_test`, `t_identity_test_refuses_production`, `t_identity_relabel_breaks_mac` |
kernel/native/m5/README.md	Refuses wrong key, any binding mismatch, source == destination, and a counter not above the last accepted one.
kernel/native/m5/README.md	Tests use freshly generated random keys named `TEST-ONLY-*` and delete them; the tool test also cross-checks the public key and the signature against openssl when it is installed (an outside tool used only as a test oracle, never linked).
kernel/native/m5/README.md	The secret key is read only from the given path (32 raw bytes, 64 hex digits or unencrypted Ed25519 PKCS#8 DER, mode 0600 or tighter), so the real signature can be made only in the offline Gate 3 ceremony, for example after `openssl pkey -in owner_root.pem -outform DER -out <file on tmpfs>`.
kernel/native/m5/README.md	The whole header (total length, envelope id, key generation) sits in every chunk's AAD and the key is per object, so reorder, truncate, extend, header edits and splices across objects, kinds, stores, classes or key generations fail.
kernel/native/m5/README.md	Verification refuses a wrong length (BOUNDS), bad magic/version/flags/reserved/class (FORMAT, even under a valid signature), another class (IDENTITY), another key, a key id that does not match the trusted key, an unsigned or tampered record (AUTH), any field the verifier does not expect or source == destination (BINDING), and a counter not above the last accepted one (REPLAY).

kernel/native/net/aienos_sec.h	Key schedule (HKDF-SHA256, RFC 5869, one 32-byte output block each): dh = X25519(eph_sk, peer_eph) (all-zero result refused) PRK = HKDF-Extract(salt = SHA-256("AIENOS-M6B-v1 hkdf salt"), dh) fin_key_r = Expand(PRK, "fin r" | TH_a), fin_key_i = Expand(PRK, "fin i" | TH_a) master = Expand(PRK, "master" | TH_f) key_i2r[0] = Expand(master, "i2r"), key_r2i[0] = Expand(master, "r2i") session_id = Expand(master, "session")[0..8) key_dir[e + 1] = Expand(key_dir[e], "rekey") (old key wiped)
kernel/native/net/aienos_sec.h	our Ed25519 identity secret key (TEST only here)
kernel/native/net/aienos_sec.h	The receiver keeps a SEC_WINDOW-record sliding replay window (accepts reordering inside it, refuses duplicates and anything older) and the keys of the current and previous epoch only.
kernel/native/net/aienos_virtio_net.c	Callers only pass constant offsets that vnet_init checked against the window lengths up front; the check here is a second line.
kernel/native/net/aienos_virtio_net.c	Defense in depth: the posted[] check below also catches every case the tests construct.
kernel/native/net/aienos_virtio_net.c	Refuses (returns 0) when the buffer would leave the region (cannot happen for a correctly sized region; kept as a guard).
kernel/native/net/aienos_virtio_net.h	Every other feature is declined (no packed ring, no indirect descriptors, no event index, no offloads, no mergeable RX buffers, no control queue).
kernel/native/net/aienos_virtio_net.h	The driver builds every descriptor itself from the descriptor id (never reads a descriptor back from shared memory) and checks it lies inside the region.
kernel/native/net/aienos_virtio_net.h	With VNET_INIT_REQUIRE_ACCESS_PLATFORM the driver refuses (fail closed, before FEATURES_OK) a device that does not offer it: an IOMMU-confined caller must never run a device that would bypass the IOMMU.

kernel/native/net/README.md	Real QEMU virtio-net-pci always carries a `VIRTIO_PCI_CAP_PCI_CFG` (cfg_type 5) capability with bar 0, offset 0, length 0, which the virtio 1.x spec allows, so every real QEMU virtio-net device was refused with `InvalidRegion`.
kernel/native/net/README.md	- **Record damage.** Every truncation, every extension of 1 to 64 bytes and every single-bit flip of a record is refused.
kernel/native/net/README.md	Rings and buffers live in one caller region; descriptors are rebuilt from the id on every post and checked inside the region; nothing the device writes is trusted.
kernel/native/net/README.md	- **Tampering.** A one-byte change at every position of HS1, HS2 and HS3 is refused, and no handshake completes.

kernel/native/net/tcp/gate.sh	Each changes only with what it counts, in the same commit: FUZZ_PIN the seeded frames of the run; GROUPS_PIN the case groups corpus.om runs (cgroup calls reached); CHECKS0_PIN and IDS0_PIN the checks run at fuzz 0 and the sha256 of their sorted distinct ids; MUTANTS_PIN the rows of mutants.tsv; GEN_FNS and GEN_PIN the functions mutgen.awk edits and the rows it makes of them; UNASSERTED_PIN the README's unasserted rows; CONTROLS_PIN the controls below.
kernel/native/net/tcp/gate.sh	Exit 0 only when every run passes every check.
kernel/native/net/tcp/gate.sh	Exit 3 (NOT_RUN, never a pass) when the gate cannot run: missing pin, missing or wrong compiler, missing QEMU, KVM, IMAGE or BUSYBOX.
kernel/native/net/tcp/gate.sh	Last line, exit 0 only when all of that holds: TCP_CORPUS_GATE PASS checks=N fuzz=F groups=G mutants=M killed=M generated=X gen_killed=Y survivors=Z controls=C The QEMU mode runs the corpus mode first, then boots a fresh guest per run whose only TCP/IP stack is tcpd (tcp.om + tcpd.om), routes its NIC through link.om (seeded drop / reorder / duplicate / hostile injection) to QEMU slirp, and talks to peer.om on the host.
kernel/native/net/tcp/gate.sh	Reached from a self-control copy, the gate stops here (exit 4, never a pass).
kernel/native/net/tcp/gate.sh	Then the controls: NOT_RUN for a missing or wrong compiler, and changed copies of this directory (a mutant row deleted, commented or duplicated, a case group skipped, a check deleted, an unasserted row deleted, a generated function dropped, trnd unrefused, a constant tcpd key, tcpd's short read kept) that must each fail; then every mutant in mutants.tsv must fail its own check, and of the generated single-line mutants of the congestion, RTO, ACK and FIN code exactly the README's unasserted edits may survive.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	A run passes only when the peer, the guest stack, QEMU's exit and the digest all agree (echo bytes are compared one by one by `peer.om`), and the link log proves the profile's impairments happened; a hostile run must have injected all 15 kinds.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	| `corpus.om` | Hosted hostile corpus (`cat tcp.om corpus.om \| omc`): exact-state cases, in 30 case groups, for every mechanism named in the `tcp.om` row (expected values from the RFC formulas, and SipHash outputs from OpenSSL as an independent oracle), with the payload of sent segments checked against the stream written; a seeded fuzz phase with invariants; and a final transfer whose sequence numbers wrap past 2^32.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	Every row of `mutants.tsv`, built and run at fuzz 0, must fail its own check: nonzero exit, no `CORPUS_PASS` and `CORPUS_FAIL check=ID`, or `nokey`, or `tcpdkey`.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	Exit codes in both modes: 3 NOT_RUN (never a pass) when the gate cannot run: missing pin, missing or wrong compiler, and in QEMU mode no QEMU, no access to `/dev/kvm`, or no IMAGE or BUSYBOX; 2 when a unit does not build with the pinned compiler; 1 when a check fails.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	- Hosted corpus: exercised by `gate.sh corpus` at each head that runs it; the result belongs to that head and compiler only.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	It ends with `GENERATED rows= built= killed= survived= not_built= kill_rate=` and `TCP_CORPUS_GATE PASS checks=N fuzz=F groups=G mutants=M killed=M generated=X gen_killed=Y survivors=Z controls=C`, exit 0, only when all of that holds; any other result is exit 1.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	It first checks its tables against pins in `gate.sh`: the rows of `mutants.tsv` (all well formed, no name or edit twice), the rows `mutgen.awk` makes, and the unasserted list below.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	| `mutgen.awk` | Generates single-line edits (operator, constant and condition flips, deleted statements) of the functions `gate.sh` names; `gate.sh corpus` runs all of them.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	- `tackin` 2: `st = get Tcb kST` -> `note mutant`: inside `tackin` the state changes only from syn-received to ESTABLISHED, and neither has a FIN of ours to acknowledge.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	- `tdata` 1: `if or lt st 4 gt st 6` -> `if or lt st 3 gt st 6`: syn-received never reaches `tdata` (`tackin` refuses the segment or moves the block to ESTABLISHED).
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	- `tfinacc` 1: `put Tcb kACK 1` -> `note mutant`: every caller has already set kACK for the same segment.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	- `tfinin` 2: `put Tcb kACK 1` -> `note mutant`: a FIN past the window comes only on a segment with data (a bare one is unacceptable), for which `tdata` has set kACK.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	The pin is omega-mv's own: the SHA-256 in `seed/out/omc.sha256` at the omega-mv root (kernel/ is folded into omega-mv), which `gate.sh` reads and checks; it refuses any other compiler.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	The QEMU hostile profile therefore injects only frames a correct stack must refuse in every state: on handshake segments (SYN set or ACK clear) only the malformed kinds 0 to 5, on ARP only garbage frames, because a RST with an acceptable ACK or a SYN-ACK with a foreign ISS is legal there.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	- `tpersist` 1: `if not tcansend` -> `if false`: in every state that cannot send, the next checks return (nothing unsent, or SND.UNA < SND.MAX while the SYN is outstanding).
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	- `twinupd` 1: `a = get Tpk 9` -> `a = get Tpk 10`: the SND.WL2 test decides only when SEG.SEQ = SND.WL1, and under the SND.UNA =< SEG.ACK rule SND.WL2 =< SEG.ACK always holds (RFC 9293 still states it); a value read the same way from every segment keeps it true.
kernel/native/net/tcp/README.md	With RFC 6528 numbers an off-path host cannot compute another connection's ISN from the ISNs it sees on its own without the key (the corpus checks each 4-tuple field and a Berlekamp-Massey predictor over 512 observations); an on-path host can still reset or inject, as nothing authenticates segments.
kernel/native/net/tests/ctl_test.c	A seeded simulated link drops, duplicates, corrupts and reorders frames; every scenario must deliver every message in order exactly once, or fail the link deterministically.
kernel/native/net/tests/ctl_test.c	Deterministic loss/recovery and refusal tests for the M6-A control-transport stub (TEST identity only).
kernel/native/net/tests/net_test.c	only a flip to "no checksum" passes
kernel/native/net/tests/report_send.c	report_send.c -- host tool for scripts/ck_spark_report_test.sh: builds the Spark boot report frames with native/net/aienos_report.c exactly as the kernel does (net_report_frame), checks each with net_report_parse, and sends each frame's UDP payload to 127.0.0.1:PORT, last part first and every part twice, so the listener must reorder and ignore repeats.
kernel/native/net/tests/sec_test.c	Every key here is a public TEST key (sec_test_identity); the "entropy" is a seeded generator, NOT-FOR-PRODUCTION.
kernel/native/net/tests/sec_test.c	every tampered message was refused by a machine
kernel/native/net/tests/sec_test.c	Impersonation: Mallory claims the expected identity but can only sign with her own key; she knows every ephemeral secret she uses, so the finished MAC is valid and only the signature check can refuse.
kernel/native/net/tests/sec_test.c	M6-B secure control transport tests (hosted, TEST identities only).
kernel/native/net/tests/vnet_test.c	a legacy-only device is still refused as NoVersion1 first
kernel/native/net/tests/vnet_test.c	Read the next available descriptor of queue qi; checks every field.
kernel/native/net/x25519.h	Returns 0 normally, or -1 if out is the all-zero value (u was a small-order point); callers doing key agreement must refuse that case.

kernel/native/om/lm1b/README.md	At load the program checks, in order: the file is exactly 807,694,368 bytes; the header starts with `GGUF`; the header rebuilt as text (version, every metadata key with its type and value, every tensor with its name, type, dimensions and offset, the data start) is byte-for-byte equal to `manifest.txt`, which is pinned inside the program; and the SHA-256 of the whole file equals the pinned digest.

kernel/native/store/store_sealed.c	the KeySlotManifest travels in plaintext: shape-checked here, one per transaction, admitted only as the next link of the chain (sequence count + 1 naming the bound manifest; sequence 1 with a zero previous id on a Store without one), digest-bound by the commit record exactly like an envelope
kernel/native/store/store_sealed.h	It refuses a Store-only rollback, a stale anchor more than one step behind and any forged or replayed record, but an attacker who rolls back BOTH regions together is not detected.
kernel/native/store/store_sealed.h	Unkeyed bootstrap read of the newest KeySlotManifest (kind 21) before any key exists: structural Store open into scratch, every kind-21 object shape-checked (magic, format version) and the chain 1..N verified by manifest_sequence and previous_keyslot_manifest_id; out receives the newest.

kernel/native/store/tests/torn_slot_test.c	-1 = never; else fail the Nth read
kernel/native/store/tests/torn_slot_test.c	For every crash image the test runs recovery and asserts it returns the complete old record or the complete new record, byte for byte, and never anything else.
kernel/native/store/tests/torn_slot_test.c	Read error is EIO, never a verdict about the media.
kernel/native/store/tests/torn_slot_test.c	The naive write goes through the same emulated device, trace and build_crash() as the protocol campaigns, so this control fails if the injector stops producing any one kind of tear.
kernel/native/store/torn_slot.c	The digest bytes cannot be checked this way; damage confined to them before any second commit reads as EMPTY.
kernel/README.md	GitHub keeps one required check on `main`, `AIEN Verification`, which only checks that signed result.
kernel/README.md	It is agent-native: the agent is the control plane, and the model is never the kernel.

kernel/README.md	Nothing here is qualified for real use.** The C kernel has run on a Raspberry Pi 5 and on a 2017 iMac, but only in test builds with TEST keys or throwaway secrets.

kernel/ROADMAP.md	This file is updated at every gate.
kernel/scripts/allen_exam_card.sh	Refuses the held-out file and any path under ~/sealed: this tool is for train/dev rehearsal and bounds tests; a sealed set is staged only by the declared final scoring procedure.
kernel/scripts/allen_pi_results.sh	Then prints score.sh rows for the Pi and for the committed host results (allen-reflex) and checks that every decision ("raw") is identical, case by case.
kernel/scripts/allen_route_card.sh	Capabilities never go into the card: the image passes its own (core/allen.c CK_ALLEN_ROUTE_CAPS).
kernel/scripts/allen_route_card.sh	Refuses held-out and sealed files: the Pi card is a rehearsal; held-out families are scored once on the host only.
kernel/scripts/allen_route_mutants.sh	Runs crates/allen-reflex/tests/route.rs once as is (must PASS) and once per host-only mutant feature of crates/allen-reflex (must FAIL, else the tests do not catch that mutation): route-mutant-grant an approval phrase runs directly instead of going to the owner desk route-mutant-dialog-pending pending state taken from the conversation (injectable) instead of the lifecycle route-mutant-guest-memory a guest reads owner memory Usage: scripts/allen_route_mutants.sh [cargo target dir].
kernel/scripts/build_recovery_media.sh	Read-only probes for offline tests.
kernel/scripts/build_recovery_media.sh	Reject a Unix filesystem before any privileged mount or write, and check the actual mounted type after mounting.
kernel/scripts/build_recovery_media.sh	Resolve aliases before the block check, all identity probes and the mount.
kernel/scripts/build_standalone_recovery_initrd.sh	gocryptfs is the only way to open the private storage and the Forgejo store from the rescue shell (Gate 1 items 11-12): a stick without it is not a recovery stick, so fail loudly unless the caller explicitly accepts that.
kernel/scripts/build_standalone_recovery_initrd.sh	Mirror the report to the serial port too, so emulator tests can read it; bounded so a serial port with no carrier can never delay the shell.
kernel/scripts/cap_abi_isolation_test.sh	---- build both archives (any failure here is a test failure) -------------
kernel/scripts/cap_abi_isolation_test.sh	It builds both archives and checks, with nm and the linker: 1 both archives define their expected entry points (non-empty, positive) 2 no aienos_cap* text symbol is defined by both archives 3 the C archive defines no aienos_cap32_*; the Rust one no aienos_cap_* 4 a C program compiled against aienos_capability.h links and runs against the C archive, and FAILS TO LINK against the Rust archive with an undefined reference to aienos_cap_* 5 control: a C caller of the cap32 ABI links against the Rust archive with the same link line, and aienos_cap32_abi_layout reports 8/4/4/64, v1 6 the C layout asserts reject a drifted header: a narrowed generation, and an equal-size reorder of AienosCapRef Any build failure, or any check that cannot run, fails the test (exit 1).
kernel/scripts/cap_abi_isolation_test.sh	This test proves they cannot be swapped by accident.
kernel/scripts/capture_gate1_receipt.sh	hardware Never checked here.
kernel/scripts/capture_gate1_receipt.sh	Nothing in the receipt is assumed: every field is either measured here or marked NOT_RUN.

kernel/scripts/capture_gate1_receipt.sh	What it checks: host The recovery image (a gzip'd cpio archive) opens, has /init, and carries every recovery tool the build script packages.

kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	A: all present gates PASS; the store child also claims PASS for gates with no C implementation, which must stay NOT_RUN.
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	A missing child script, a child that reports NOT_RUN, a child that prints no verdict line, or a PASS line contradicted by the child's exit status is never PASS.
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	A selected run never claims the whole set passed.
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	B: boot FAIL; store script missing -> its three gates NOT_RUN, never PASS.
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	------------------------------------------------------------ child scripts set_default_children: the real child scripts (only main calls this; the self-test points the same variables at canned scripts in a temp folder).
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	Confined is reported only from the confined PASS line and the negative-test PASS line.
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	Dirty-tree refusal, end to end, on a private clone (never this tree).
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	D: no marker at all (exit 0); PASS lines with an unexplained exit 2.
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	Every other gate prints AIENOS_CK_<gate>: NOT_SELECTED (never PASS, never dropped); an unknown name is refused, exit 2.
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	Gate selection (--only): empty selection means every gate (the default).
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	Gates with no C implementation print NOT_RUN (MISSING_IMPLEMENTATION: <reason>) and never run anything.

keys from QEMU sendkey) fpu -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_fpu_test.sh (NOT_RUN without the Rust target) infer -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_infer_test.sh (NOT_RUN without the model file or the Rust target) screen -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_screen_test.sh (GOP framebuffer console vs a QMP screendump) console -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_console_test.sh (C-only: sustained serial + USB console session in a TEST-ONLY QEMU image; NOT_RUN on gate lock or quiet flag) rollback -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_rollback_test.sh (C image as the one-time BootNext candidate, AAVMF) osc -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_osc_unit_test.sh (OSC unit admission, OSC_UNIT_ARTIFACT v1; QEMU, TEST signer, not physical) osc_launch -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_osc_launch_test.sh (OSC unit task launch, OSC_UNIT_ARTIFACT v1 section 9; QEMU, TEST signer, not physical) osc_receipt -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_osc_receipt_test.sh (launch requires a valid Omega realization receipt, aienos#383; QEMU, TEST signer, not physical) osh_core -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_osh_core_test.sh (OSH shell core as EL0 tasks, step trace equals Linux; QEMU, TEST signer, not physical) osh_op -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_osh_op_test.sh (a real capability-authorized OSH operation, klog; QEMU, TEST signer, not physical) slots -> verdict line from scripts/qemu_ck_slot_loader_test.sh (SLOTLDR.EFI signed A/B slot loader: bounded tries, rollback, refusals; QEMU, TEST signer, not physical) missing -> NOT_RUN (MISSING_IMPLEMENTATION), never run, never PASS ===========================================================================
kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	K: SMP child FAIL (a core never checked in) -> FAIL; L: quiet flag held (exit 3) -> NOT_RUN; M: PASS line with an unexplained exit 2 -> FAIL; N: only the mutation verdict line (AIENOS_CK_SMP_MUTATION) -> no SMP verdict -> FAIL, never PASS.

kernel/scripts/ck_gates.sh	W, X, Y: KEYBOARD comes only from its own child (scripts/qemu_ck_keyboard_test.sh): PASS with exit 0 is PASS, a FAIL verdict is FAIL, exit 3 (lock or quiet flag) is NOT_RUN.
kernel/scripts/ck_imac_bsdp_test.sh	read request that repeats blksize until a chained option answer would pass the end of its 128-byte buffer, and a clock gmtime cannot convert (LD_PRELOAD time shim).
kernel/scripts/ck_imac_bsdp_test.sh	Runs the answerer on loopback high ports and plays the iMac with a small C client that sends the INFORM[LIST] the real iMac18,3 sent on 2026-10-17 (vendor class "AAPLBSDPC/i386/iMac18,3", option 43 = 01 01 01 02 02 01 01) plus a reply port, then an INFORM[SELECT] for the offered image; checks every answer field; then fetches the booter with curl over TFTP (blksize + tsize) and compares bytes; checks a wrong file name and a DHCP DISCOVER get nothing.
kernel/scripts/ck_imac_bsdp_test.sh	The server is built with ASan + UBSan, so any write past a buffer ends the test.
kernel/scripts/ck_imac_lab_services_test.sh	The cases: an unrelated live pid in a pid file is not ours (status says not running, stop leaves it alive); a started stub is recognised, start again does not launch a second one, and a deleted binary does not matter; a changed start time or boot id, a pid file from an older launcher older than the process, a dead pid, a pid whose program has another name, and junk or zero in the pid file are all refused; an older launcher's pid file for the real stub is still accepted; a foreign receiver on UDP 47031 is left alone.
kernel/scripts/ck_imac_report_test.sh	Host checks of x86/report_x86.c: the allowlist filter (sentinel chat, key, token and memory lines, control bytes and over-long lines never reach the exported copy or a frame; allowed lines survive byte for byte) and the frames (Ethernet broadcast, IPv4, UDP with a checksum, "v=2 auth=none" part headers), and the late-diagnostics boundary: an allowlisted line written after 16 KiB of console survives, conversation lines around it do not, and a full console copy ends in a truncation note.
kernel/scripts/ck_imac_report_test.sh	The receiver tools/ck_imac_report_listen.c rebuilds the report byte for byte from parts sent out of order and repeated (and a full 64-part report), labels it unauthenticated, and refuses every older header (the retired v=1 raw-console format and the old name).
kernel/scripts/ck_imac_security_test.sh	ck_imac_security_test.sh -- regression tests for the iMac security fixes (L872): F1 the wizard's API key must never be queued on, nor stay in, the unencrypted Ethernet courier; F2 the kernel must verify the owner's signature before it writes a boot section; F3 nor may it install an owner-signed kernel below the installed kernel's policy_generation (an old kernel replayed over the unauthenticated network loader).
kernel/scripts/ck_imac_security_test.sh	No full-model QEMU, no disk, no network, no private key (RFC 8032 public test vectors only).

kernel/scripts/gate_hold_selftest.sh	---- 2: kill mid-gate, then cleanup removes only its own dead hold
kernel/scripts/gate_hold_selftest.sh	---- 8: no script writes or removes the machine quiet flag trust1_m5_qualify.sh is left out of the grep: it writes and removes a flag only in --self-test, after pointing QUIET_FLAG at a temp file; 8b checks that every reassignment of its QUIET_FLAG is such a temp file or a restore.
kernel/scripts/gate_hold_selftest.sh	Cases: 1 two competing gates: the second refuses (exit 3) and names the holder 2 a gate killed mid-run (SIGKILL, no trap): another gate does not remove that dead hold before its expected_end; the same gate removes its own dead hold and runs; a dead hold past expected_end is stale for any gate 3 a record without a readable pid (old "<name> <pid>" or "lane tag" form) is held and never removed, in the gate lock and in the quiet flag 4 the quiet flag held by a live owner: the gate refuses by name, the flag is unchanged 5 the quiet flag of the caller's own quietlock hold (hold=<id> equal to QUIETLOCK_HOLD): the gate runs and leaves the flag alone 6 release removes only this run's own record 7 the gate lock record is in the quietlock record format (holder, start=, expected_end=, pid=, hold=); when quietlock is installed it reads the holder as alive 8 no script under scripts/ writes or removes the machine quiet flag 9 a "*" in a record is not globbed; a reused pid (other start time) is dead; no date or no flock: the gate refuses and writes nothing Exit 0 when every case passes, 1 otherwise.
